# Shared documentation drift

Find accumulated documentation problems across a repository, then turn verified proposals into one reviewable change. The shared operation has audit and resolve modes.

For example, an old guide links to a moved source file. The local scanner flags the link; the shared workflow reads the guide and current source before proposing the correction. A stale audit PR gets checked again before its change is reused.

| Shared in vh-adlc | Retained in the adopting repository |
| --- | --- |
| Audit and resolution procedure, finding dispositions and concise reports | Scanner implementation, roots, detector exceptions and thresholds |
| Recheck proposed edits against current source and preserve human decisions | Documentation styles, generated indexes, site and visual checks |
| Distinguish a wrong detector from a wrong interpretation | Suppressions, corrections history, schedules and publication permissions |

The scope excludes claim-by-claim docs-truth sweeps and their rolling ledger. Existing docs impact continues to handle the documentation affected by one selected code change.

## Context

### Owner summary

Move Stackweaver's scheduled documentation audit and stale audit-PR resolver into shared vh-adlc. Keep the scanner, detector rules, feedback, generators, site checks, schedule, credentials and project delivery policy in Stackweaver. Preserve complete collection, protected specs/history, independent review, concise reports, remembered findings, bot/owner publishing and recovery that verifies remote state before replay.

The proposed simplification uses the ordinary installed plugin and native clients, shared process/review primitives and one run directory. Callers use eight helper operations. Scanner protocol version 1 and its separate completeness correction remain required. Recovery keeps original account ownership, partial-delivery refusal and evidence-backed owner acknowledgement. Shared execution work must preserve existing loop/triage behavior.

Three operational changes need acceptance. Q1 uses ordinary installed clients. Changes to plugin revision, configured role provider/command/model, source/read set, config, candidate, payload, authority or publisher identity need fresh review. Native client, Bun, Git and gh version strings are diagnostic when their required capabilities and observed model still match. Q2 replaces automatic remote restoration after lost local application evidence with observation and an owner-reviewed manual cleanup handoff; the helper keeps an unproved lease pending. Q3 replaces adoption receipt chains with a committed staged/adopted gate, released component binding and acyclic preflight checklist. Each decision is independent.

Stackweaver's legacy routes stay active through staged testing. Migration uses a recorded checklist of exact release/install, scanner parity, Claude Code Opus 5.5 paired review, actual model/entry tests, a successful unattended installed synthetic trial, routing switch and rollback. The trial needs its separately selected provider and exact owned-fixture effect authority. Real-corpus scanner parity remains a separate read-only check. The current code still implements the earlier architecture. Its recorded tests and reviews keep their original commit attribution. This revision has no implementation or migration acceptance yet. GUI and phone checks retain their deferrals.

Acceptance covers the revised implementation plan and the installation, lost-evidence recovery and adoption choices the owner selects. Existing publication, provider-execution, private-payload and cutover boundaries still govern those later actions.

### Simplification basis on 2026-11-15

At published `b7b85398eff501083824a7f2b48032dcd1026f88`, PR #98 adds 50,862 lines across 303 files. The measured additions include 18,967 runtime/tooling lines in 109 files and 25,895 test/fixture lines. Actual Claude Code Opus 5.5 architecture diagnostic reads all 203 supplied fragments, including 28 complete core modules, normative design and complete module inventories. It identifies useful domain controls and excessive execution/adoption infrastructure. The diagnostic is archived as `docs-drift-architecture-claude-r1`, with exact source, original return and qualified model/read/cleanup records. It is distinct from canonical spec acceptance.

Current reuse opportunities and limits are visible in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts:60`, `scripts/loop/review-route.ts:20` and `:204`. The two model matchers at `scripts/loop/review-route.ts:198` and `scripts/docs-drift/manifests.ts:220` accept different alias families, so reuse must preserve caller semantics. Existing retained runtime gates are in `scripts/docs-drift/bootstrap.ts:465`, `adoption.ts:602` and `role-capture.ts:96`. The old 738-line command surface starts its command groups at `cli.ts:324`. These are implementation observations, not proof that a simpler replacement already works.

Merge `a9be541` integrates upstream `3e3f655`, including the ordinary 0.32.0 binary lock and loop fixes. `git show origin/main:plugins/vh-adlc/plugin.json` at that upstream declares 0.24.1. The merge resolves both plugin manifests to candidate 0.25.0. The retained working-tree package/discovery output passes 69 cases and 301 assertions in `simplification-upstream-package-check.log`. The log itself has no source/exit/clean-state record, so it supplies no frozen-source attribution. The owner requested retaining useful capabilities and reducing disproportionate machinery on 2026-11-15.

The architecture diagnostic's suggestions to drop delivery acknowledgement, compare numeric account IDs alone and split this port into additional feature documents are declined. The owner selected the useful audit/resolver/recovery capabilities as one migration, including original-account handling and acknowledgement.

### Review lens

Architecture proportionality is a should-fix lens for this revision and its implementation review. Every retained subsystem needs a concrete audit/resolver, recovery or migration behavior and an explanation where an existing shared primitive cannot preserve that behavior. This lens favors reuse or removal when they satisfy the retained outcome. It introduces no line-count target or requirement to weaken correctness checks.

### Terms used here

| Term | Meaning | Existing criterion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Docs-drift audit | Account for the declared corpus and ground each assessed finding in current source. | AC-01, AC-02 |
| Resolve | Recheck selected proposals and record the current disposition of every selected hunk. | AC-04 |
| Prepare | Apply selected eligible corrections to an owned candidate under current permission. | AC-03, AC-05 |
| Advisory | An independently reviewed proposed PR awaiting the project's normal merge decision. | AC-05 |
| New attention | A new or materially changed unresolved decision or verification item. | AC-05 |
| Carried attention | An unresolved item whose current binding still matches eligible earlier evidence. | AC-05 |
| Carried delivery | A freshly supported correction already present in an exact open reviewed proposal. | AC-05 |
| Delivery acknowledgement | Current owner approval to preserve a proven applied delivery's later edits and stop original replay, retaining non-success. | AC-05 |
| Run directory | One private external directory holding the run's inputs, review, approved plan, journal and report. | AC-05, AC-07 |
| Migration checklist | The bound preflight record of release/install/synthetic trial/parity/final review/activation plan/rollback rehearsal, followed by a separate post-switch report. | AC-06, AC-07 |
| Adoption | The reviewed D activation of staged candidate C under its component binding, complete preflight checklist and exact activation diff. | AC-06 |
| Synthetic test | A hermetic fake-service or scratch-namespace check that grants no real-corpus execution. | AC-07 |
| Unattended trial | A separately authorized scheduled-style execution of installed code and actual model routes against the committed synthetic fixture and exact owned namespace. | AC-06, AC-07 |

#### Native closure request cost

The timed serial closure fixture records 1,465 native-service calls, including 1,310 REST requests, before its process limit. Its last request is 0.23 seconds before the timeout record, showing continuing collection. GitHub documents a usual built-in Actions-token allowance of 1,000 REST requests per repository per hour. T105 reproduces that ordinary hosted constraint in an owned fixture and corrects repeated collection. The interrupted larger-window diagnostic and all timeout attempts remain in the review archive. T104 was completed after the corrected T105 checkpoint. The source is `t104-normal-delivery-serial-checkpoint-closure-publish-resume.log` and the preserved fixture's `native-service-calls.jsonl`. [GitHub REST rate limits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api)

#### Local history retention

Local invocation metadata uses the explicitly selected private runtime cache. `reports.max_runs` bounds the saved report pairs inspected, and `max_age_seconds` bounds eligibility. New records are indexed by invocation digest; the timestamp-addressed format remains readable. Each metadata file is byte-bounded. Completed metadata and interrupted `.pending-*` files stay in the owner-controlled cache until explicit cleanup, preserving recovery evidence. T24 and T27 carry these setup and retention instructions into the operator guides. Current implementation is in `scripts/docs-drift/local-history.ts`, `stageLocalInvocation` and `createLocalInvocationReader`, together with `report-history.ts`, `collectLocalHistory`.

#### Corpus-wide finding compatibility

The consumer can emit corpus-wide findings with no single document. The shared finding validator previously required a document path and rejected that input. T107 restores this input range before the consumer protocol correction. A corpus finding retains its scope and current source-evidence obligation. Document-bound findings retain their document quote requirement; actual edit protection and publication authority remain separately enforced.

T29 source inspection also identifies a native-provider gap. The prior role builder, process wrapper and stream decoder permitted Copilot only, while the accepted migration preserves Stackweaver's Claude scheduled scanning provider. T109 adds that missing source path before staged consumer acceptance. This does not authorize an actual provider/model trial or a scanning-provider change. The local Claude CLI reports version 2.1.325; its read-only help supplies the supported command flags. OAuth-capable configuration must remain usable because the existing scheduled workflow uses that credential path.

#### Historical glossary and context

| Term | Meaning | Existing criterion |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Docs-drift audit | Account for the declared documentation corpus, report candidates and distinguish observed verification from unknowns. | AC-01, AC-02 |
| Prepare | Make only the selected eligible corrections in an owned candidate under the current permission. | AC-03, AC-05 |
| Resolve | Recheck selected proposals and feedback against current source, then classify every selected change. | AC-04 |
| Advisory | The exact independently reviewed PR handed off for the project's normal review and merge process. | AC-05 |
| New attention | A new or materially changed unresolved decision or verification item requiring owner input. | AC-05 |
| Carried attention | An unresolved item whose complete current binding matches eligible earlier evidence. | AC-05 |
| Carried delivery | A freshly supported correction already present in the exact open reviewed proposal. | AC-05 |
| Delivery acknowledgement | Explicitly approved non-success recovery disposition for a proven applied delivery whose current payload differs. | AC-05 |
| Adoption | The recorded, verified switch to the shared execution route under the consumer's scoped authority. | AC-06 |
| Trial | A separately authorized synthetic observation of the required installed and hosted execution paths. | AC-06, AC-07 |

The ordinary native reader hashes request method, hostname, endpoint and status, excluding per-request timestamps, at `plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/transport.ts:435`. The full feature matrix, final gate and migration acceptance remain open.

## Scope

Deliver one shared documentation-drift workflow with audit, selected-proposal resolution and post-merge cleanup. Preserve complete collection, source-grounded assessment, independent review, concise reports, rejected-claim feedback, scheduled and interactive publisher identities, and safe recovery from interrupted writes.

Keep project scanners, detector thresholds, documentation roots, historical exceptions, feedback stores, generators, site checks, schedules, credentials and delivery permissions in each adopter. Existing change-scoped docs impact and exhaustive docs truth retain their own procedures.

Use the ordinary plugin installation and shared execution primitives. Replace this draft's private client-distribution installer, retained execution caches, adoption receipt chains, upgrade classification and historical trial/adoption glue with the setup and migration checks below. Keep the installed synthetic collector/harness needed to exercise actual installed code and model routes in the owned fixture namespace. Existing local test artifacts remain evidence of their recorded revisions.

## Design

### User workflow and local configuration

The canonical skill selects audit, resolve or post-merge cleanup from the owner's request. An ambiguous request gets one scope clarification. Audit inventories the corpus and proposes confirmed corrections. Resolve rechecks selected audit PRs against current source and combines their supported changes. Post-merge cleanup closes selected originals only after verifying the merged replacement and each original hunk's disposition.

Stackweaver keeps its existing scanner and project companion. The scanner protocol remains version 1, including raw-byte document accounting and required execution-unit accounting. The earlier separate scanner-completeness correction remains a prerequisite for adoption. The shared implementation continues using hardened Git reads, the existing strict YAML reader and docs-impact coverage matching. Current entry points are `scan.ts:187`, `snapshot.ts:248` and `settings.ts:321` and `settings.ts:453` at the implementation checkpoint recorded in Context.

The replacement committed configuration describes repository/host/default branch, documentation scope, required detector selectors/options, scanner arguments, feedback/procedure paths, generators/checks, proposal discovery, publisher accounts, report history, selected native role commands and existing credential-reference names. It uses the shared strict YAML reader. Unknown fields, incomplete setup and unreadable policy get an actionable setup result before provider execution or writes. Its schema distinguishes the replacement configuration from the unreleased staged format. Scanner protocol version 1 remains independently stable.

The normal entry is harness-agnostic. The installed skill and one shared helper serve local and scheduled callers. A scheduled wrapper supplies its repository policy, native client commands and existing secret mappings. Host capabilities and configured routes decide which actual model operations are available. A host that only supports scanning/reporting may still use those read-only operations while execution support remains visibly unclaimed.

### Installation and role execution

Use the ordinary marketplace installation, plugin revision and verified binary installer. Record the actual plugin revision, native client version, Bun version, requested model, observed model and operation target in the run. The adopter provisions its normal client and credentials. Setup checks required commands/capabilities before work. CI continues using the declared toolchain. Publication uses the selected validated run inputs without repeating an exact Bun-version equality check for every effect.

The approved component binding is distinct from the engineering docs revision passed as `--plugin-pin`. The consumer records the released plugin version, released source commit and existing component payload fingerprint. Extract/reuse `componentDigest` and the byte/inventory checks from `inspectRetainedPayload` in `bootstrap.ts` for the ordinary installed directory. Its existing private-cache ownership/0700/nlink policy is inapplicable to marketplace installation and must not be imposed on or used to chmod that installation. Resolve its actual root and verify the released regular-file inventory, executable modes and bytes without creating another copy. The fingerprint is SHA-256 of canonical JSON containing UTF-8-sorted `[relative_path, Git_mode, SHA256(raw_bytes)]` rows for the published plugin files. Compare the ordinary installed payload to that released inventory once per invocation. A source-file change under the same version fails the binding. The docs revision remains independently pinned.

An installed plugin update takes effect through ordinary installation and the adopter's reviewed configuration. A pending run carries its original input and tool-version record. Changes to plugin revision, configured role provider/command/requested or observed model, source/read set, configuration, candidate, payload, authority or publisher identity require a fresh independent review and applicable current owner/procedure approval. Native client, Bun, Git and gh version strings are diagnostic only when setup capability checks and the recorded model binding still match. A resume that invokes no role compares the current configured provider/command/requested model with the original observed model and original role configuration. It performs no new provider call or model probe solely for a diagnostic version change. An actual new role invocation validates its newly observed native model/context. A failed capability/identity check remains pending. A version-only patch difference cannot by itself invalidate completed semantic review. If the original reviewed inputs cannot be established, retain the pending run and prepare a freshly reviewed plan from observed current state. Missing execution evidence is an explicit handoff.

Reuse the existing bounded executor in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts:60` for native role processes. Its current contract lacks watched output-file bounds, bounded in-memory capture with a drain deadline, startup-callback cancellation, cancellation handling and output limits. Add only these needed options with unchanged defaults and existing-caller regressions. Move every current caller together before removing the duplicate executor, including scan, proposal fixed-ref fetch, native GitHub/Git mutations, preparation commands, candidate verification, role processes, history downloads and installed trial publication. Retired client version probes need no replacement caller. Native mutation responses stay in bounded memory and only their safe projections reach disk. Cancellation/timeout must clean the owned process tree and preserve failed captures. Never kill processes by executable name.

Reuse compatible execution and identity primitives from `scripts/loop/review-route.ts:204`. Its current API requires a feature document and spec/implementation review kind. Documentation assessment retains its own target and response format. Share a lower-level primitive where both real callers need it, while preserving the lifecycle review API and outcomes. Do not fabricate a feature document to run a documentation review. Existing model matchers have different alias policies, so preserve each caller's accepted/rejected identities when consolidating shared code. Lifecycle reviews retain their current PID-namespace requirement. Docs-drift gains marker-based cleanup confirmation from the shared executor. Scanner, role, mutation, fetch, verification/preparation and history results require confirmed cleanup with zero surviving owned processes. The old docs-drift implementation checked process-group exit/reaping without this confirmation. Record the actual marker or PID boundary and claim only its observed guarantees.

Worker and reviewer run in separate contexts against recorded targets. Preserve the configured worker/reviewer providers, models and credential ownership, including the consumer's existing Claude OAuth flow. A reviewer gets read-only tools. Validate its observed native model/context, successful process completion and exact source/candidate or payload target. Unsupported/mismatched identities, timeouts, errors and changed targets leave review pending. A fresh context or a configured model name alone cannot establish an independent successful review.

### Collection, assessment and protected documents

Enumerate expected documents and detector inputs from the selected committed Git tree. Compare scanner-reported inventories, raw-byte digests and per-unit outcomes with those independently derived sets. Missing/duplicate documents, skipped reads, incomplete/throwing units, changed inputs and unreadable feedback leave the assessment incomplete. Complete empty results remain distinct from missing output.

Run trusted scanner code in its owned source copy with ambient provider/publisher credentials, Git credential helpers, askpass hooks and agent sockets excluded. Preserve the caller's logical state, including HEAD, its branch/owned refs, staged index entries, tracked/untracked work and configuration. Extend the shared logical snapshot with an optional ignored-content policy, preserving its current default for lifecycle callers. Docs-drift excludes ignored build/cache content and adds a memory-only digest of effective repository-local Git configuration. Keep values out of artifacts. Use one entry/exit comparison per invocation. Stat-only index refreshes, object repacking, maintenance metadata and changes confined to ignored build/cache files do not count as semantic writes. Tracked or nonignored untracked work and local Git configuration remain protected. Actual file/index/ref changes still fail preservation checks. Keep all writes/fetches in explicit owned copies/object stores, outside the caller's Git objects. Protect foreign resources through their recorded ownership. Scanner messages, document prose and PR text provide evidence and confer no execution authority.

Read the document and relevant source before confirming each candidate. Record confirmed drift, still accurate or unknown with its reason and evidence. Preserve unknown and unassessed items. Product defects get a separate proposed repair or issue. An intended contract, historical record or deliberately incomplete compatibility matrix remains meaningful even when its implementation is incomplete.

Preserve standard feature-document normative sections, review returns, criterion proof, completed-task history and dated provenance byte-for-byte. A claimed feature document gets a handoff to its writer. Ordinary documents owned by an active feature need coordination before edits. A local-format plan permits changes only in ranges its current procedure identifies as editable. Missing or ambiguous protection policy makes preparation incomplete. Continue using the binary's actual freshness result for review staleness.

Report-only work saves its report externally. Authorized preparation changes only selected confirmed corrections in its owned candidate. Structural changes, moves, deletion, document merges, lifecycle decisions and issue creation keep their existing explicit approval requirements. Generators and site/asset checks use consumer commands.

### Resolution, feedback and reports

Collect complete native proposal identity/body and full Git diffs, including original/current heads and base objects. Recheck each hunk and record apply, modified apply, already fixed, wrong or deferred with current evidence. Preserve contradictory proposals as decisions until adjudicated. A newer PR alone cannot establish correctness.

A detector error produces a narrowly scoped suppression. An incorrect interpretation produces correction history. Preserve a real detector finding when its interpretation was wrong. Suppression retirement requires the corresponding complete execution unit and scope. Read prior rejected claims before proposing them again.

Keep a resolution record linking original proposal/hunk identities, decisions, selected edits and the verified candidate. The independent review covers the exact proposed changes and selected publication payloads. A materially changed source, candidate, target, reviewer configuration or payload needs fresh review. A disjoint default-branch advance may proceed only when the recorded read set and relevant native inputs remain valid.

Reports start with a short explanation, applied/proposed fixes, unresolved decisions and recommendations. Detailed evidence remains accessible from the report. Required missing observations and failed execution remain visible. A complete scheduled run containing only unchanged previously reported items succeeds while listing all unresolved items and owned open proposals. New attention, incomplete collection, failed review or pending publication/recovery remains non-successful.

Keep both scheduled and interactive history. Read scheduled reports through the existing authenticated GitHub reader/native artifact-download facility, selecting an exact repository, workflow, branch, event, run attempt and artifact. Keep the existing bounded ZIP reader and locked vendored dependency for history ingestion. Before materializing an entry, validate its path, unique name, regular-file kind, entry count, declared/actual per-entry size and aggregate expanded bytes. Traversal, symlinks/nonregular entries, duplicates and false sizes fail closed. The authenticated reader selects bounded archive bytes and the existing reader validates them. This retains the current installed-payload behavior without adding a system unzip dependency or a new extractor. Validate the report schema and provenance after bounded extraction. Interactive reports use an owner-private external run directory and bounded index. Repository identity, stable finding IDs, evidence changes and eligible proposal references govern carry-forward. Invalid, mismatched or incomplete required history prevents a green carry-forward claim. History grants no write authority and never transfers an interrupted run to another publisher.

### One run directory and a smaller helper interface

Store one private external run directory with a versioned manifest, source/read-set/config identity, candidate and decisions, original role results, exact approved effects, journal and report. Paths remain external to caller repositories and Git directories. Validate bounded regular files on read. Keep credentials and raw secret-bearing environments out of artifacts. Digests remain internal integrity checks over the values they actually describe.

The public helper operations are scan, report, resolve, prepare, verify, publish, resume and close. Each uses the run directory, with explicit source/proposal selection at creation and explicit current approval for writes. Audit and resolution preparation remain observable stages with distinct permissions. Diagnostic output can expose an operation's pending stage without requiring callers to assemble dozens of path/hash argument pairs. `close` performs post-merge closure review and execution under its separate scope.

Private stage functions may remain when they make code and tests easier to understand. A new command or reusable abstraction requires a concrete caller and a retained user-visible need. Existing lower-level unreleased CLI commands can be removed after inventorying development consumers and replacing the staged adopter's calls and tests. Legacy Stackweaver entry points remain available until adoption.

### Publication, concurrency and recovery

Use one exact reviewed plan of ordered effects. Each effect records its native target, expected preimage, desired payload, publisher account and stable correlation marker. Persist intent before sending a mutation and record the settled outcome afterwards. Record bounded native resource IDs and applied fields needed to distinguish complete, partial and unknown delivery. Preserve the original source/plan bytes while comparing native observations separately.

Keep a repository-scoped compare-and-swap lease for cross-host writers. Actions concurrency supplements that lease. The run records the lease's exact owner and expected ref value. The run records its origin host stamp and canonical directory. Obtain the host stamp from the platform machine ID/UUID, retain only its digest and fail to observation-only if that identity cannot be established. Normal resume requires the same host and directory. Within that origin directory, reuse the revision-CAS attempt claim immediately before dispatch, so concurrent resumers cannot send the same effect. A copied run or another host gets observation-only inspection and no automatic lease takeover. Moving execution requires a separately reviewed owner-approved takeover after the original writer and requests are proven settled; its CAS binds the exact observed lease value. Unknown activity stays pending. A plain copied journal or run nonce cannot establish a new executing controller. A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes. Normal release accounts for every effect and releases only the exact owned lease. Never infer ownership from branch or resource naming.

Before each write, recheck current permission, native repository/publisher identity, source/read-set freshness, target preimage and review/payload validity. Validate immutable local inputs once into the run and reuse them during that invocation. Changes to live inputs stop the plan. Preserve whole-operation original ownership even when the current publisher map changes.

On retry, observe native state by exact resource identity and marker before replay. A uniquely matching complete effect is recorded without repeating it. An absent effect may execute once if the original reviewed plan and current authority still apply. Partial body/label/draft changes remain pending. Multiple matches, a foreign account, conflicting edits or unavailable observations remain pending. Stale review never authorizes a new write.

Owner-approved acknowledgement remains available through resume. It requires a settled original mutation, direct evidence that all approved fields applied, exact original publisher/resource bindings and a fresh approval of the current changed resource and remaining cleanup. Record it as a terminal decision that preserves the changed resource and prevents original business replay. Keep its non-success report and retain the lease while another effect remains unresolved. Use the same plan, journal and bounded native observations. Remove the parallel adoption/receipt restoration machinery after its simpler replacement has equivalent tests for these retained outcomes.

Local journal loss has a practical recovery limit. Observe current GitHub state, retain uncertainty and require a freshly reviewed/approved replacement plan when original application evidence is unavailable. Automatic reconstruction from remote receipt refs and retained historical runtime caches is retired. The helper does not clear a lease after loss of the original local application/settlement records. It reports the exact observed lease object and resource state for an explicit owner-reviewed manual cleanup outside the helper. A stopped client, timeout or two absence reads cannot prove an accepted remote request will never apply. Unknown ownership or request settlement therefore stays pending in the helper. Manual cleanup requires its own exact owner authorization and records non-success abandonment, without inventing application proof. A newly reviewed business plan accounts for observed current resources after that separate cleanup. Q2 makes this practical limit explicit. Ordinary reports and original failed artifacts remain available. No missing proof is reconstructed or treated as success.

Publisher selection supports the scheduled bot and interactive owner. Acquire the authenticated viewer's numeric ID, resolve it through the native REST account endpoint and retain the REST login. This handles the same bot appearing as a GraphQL slug and REST bracketed login. Compare configured/native login case-insensitively with exact numeric IDs. Every configured publisher must appear in explicit discovery authors, and a discovery prefix must cover its publication branch prefix. Keep recorded evidence unchanged through case-only later observations. A material login or numeric-ID change stops the old operation. A different authorized publisher may read eligible history and begin a freshly reviewed operation, while original-operation recovery stays bound to its original account.

### Migration, compatibility and rollout

The public shared feature remains on its owning branch. Its previous code and checked tasks are implementation history. Earlier test results keep their original commit attribution. This normative revision resets live acceptance proof and requires a fresh spec review and acceptance before implementation.

The staged Stackweaver branch still contains the old configuration and trial entry. Inventory its callers and any real retained runs before replacing unreleased formats. Preserve its separate scanner correction, detector behavior, legacy JSON/console/exits, feedback files, local procedure, existing Claude command, Monday 09:00 UTC schedule, secret mappings and notifications. Record exact shared/consumer revisions. A required old pending run gets an explicit retention/review handoff before cleanup.

Replace the historical adoption/upgrade receipt chains with one preflight checklist and a small committed mode gate. Keep `publication.mode` as staged/adopted and an approved component binding. Before every production write, adopted mode, the installed payload fingerprint/version match and a complete preflight checklist must pass. A checklist is operator/reviewer evidence with validated schema and source/payload bindings. It grants no write authority and claims no resistance to malicious same-user forgery.

The binding order is explicit. First review the staged consumer candidate C with its component binding, final policy and proposed activation diff. Generate the preflight checklist from C. It records C's repository/commit, component binding, required passed checks, the normalized configuration digest and the exact approved before/after activation-file hashes. Normalization omits only `publication.mode` and `publication.checklist_path`; all other settings remain bound. Commit the checklist as an immutable consumer file and activate through a subsequent reviewed commit D. D changes only the mode/checklist reference, adds that checklist and applies the recorded activation diff. The gate verifies that shape and the current normalized policy and activated files. Subsequent unrelated product changes do not invalidate the config binding. A changed policy/component needs a reviewed successor checklist under the same order.

The preflight checklist contains released-payload/install, real-corpus scanner parity, synthetic installed/model trial, final paired review of C and its activation diff, and rollback rehearsal results. It cannot contain the future actual routing-switch outcome. Observe actual switch/production outcomes after D in a separate delivery report that cannot supply missing preflight checks. This keeps hashes acyclic while retaining the staged production gate.

Upgrade checks use a fixed table. The reviewer records the applicable rows and outcomes; mixed changes take the union. Procedure/report-only changes need relevant document/package tests and installed routing smoke. Scanner/scope/feedback changes also need scanner/protocol and corpus parity. Effect/recovery changes also need source-level fake-service interruption/concurrency checks plus installed routing smoke. Provider, role model/command, credential mapping or publisher identity changes need actual affected native-entry synthetic trials. Unclassified changes remain pending for scoped review.

Publication state and installation alone cannot establish operation support.

Before consumer cutover, publish the reviewed shared dependency under its configured checks, install its actual marketplace payload in isolated profiles and exercise the selected native operations. Retain the accepted unattended installed synthetic trial. It exercises the actual installed helper, native worker/reviewer and owned fixture effects, using the committed fixture definition and a namespace disjoint from real-corpus publication. The trial harness remains test-only and cannot select production effects. Ordinary production commands cannot accept a caller-provided fake transport. Real-corpus collection/parity is a separate read-only check. Actual production use after adoption retains its selected publication authority and is reported at the observed support level. Development may use an early paired review, but AC-06 requires the final paired migration review through actual Claude Code Opus 5.5, after release/install and against the exact released component binding, final staged consumer C and reviewed activation diff. T130 produces that final proof under its exact private packet approval. Any relevant later source, consumer or activation change repeats the affected review; older results keep their source attribution. The unattended synthetic grant is a committed trial definition, selected by path/digest from staged configuration and approved before dispatch. Reuse the current definition/collector validation for fixture identity, allowed branch/resource namespace and exact effects. Bind it to the component binding, entry/role configuration and committed workflow path/source. Its owner-approved definition is the scoped standing trial permission, separate from live-corpus authority. Inside the runner, verify the native repository/workflow/actor/event/head against that definition and bind the actual run ID/attempt at launch. The grant does not need to contain its own future run ID or commit hash. Dispatch input alone cannot create it. Document this representation in the authority table.

Validate its original native model/target/context and process outcome. Automatic runtime operation reviews keep their configured provider/model/credential bindings and their own captured native identity. Keep legacy routes active until those observations and the owner's existing cutover boundary are satisfied. Final paired review includes the exact activation diff before the D switch. A disposable rollback rehearsal restores legacy routing and schedule while preserving reports and pending-operation evidence. GUI and phone checks retain their owner-approved deferrals. Other unperformed host/model surfaces remain unclaimed.

The three independent choices requiring the owner's acceptance are ordinary installed-client/version management in Q1, explicit reviewed recovery after lost local application evidence in Q2, and checklist-backed staged/adopted gating and upgrade checks in Q3. Complete collection, protected documents, independent review, both publisher identities, remembered findings and evidence-backed surviving-journal recovery remain required.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] AC-01: An adopting repository can audit documentation and resolve stale audit proposals through one discoverable harness-agnostic workflow, retaining its scanner, detector policy and existing change-scoped docs-impact behavior.
- [ ] AC-02: Complete assessments independently match scanner document/per-unit inventories and raw-byte digests to the selected Git tree and committed selectors. Failed, skipped, partial, stale or unreadable required input remains visible and prevents a clean claim. Confirmed findings and proposed fixes carry document/source evidence.
- [ ] AC-03: Authorized preparation preserves unrelated work, intended contracts, historical evidence and local feature-review rules while producing only selected verified corrections. Caller repositories and foreign resources retain their original state.
- [ ] AC-04: Resolution retains the correct detector-versus-interpretation feedback and current disposition for every original hunk. A selected original closes only after its merged consolidation and every carried or terminal disposition are freshly verified.
- [ ] AC-05: Concise reports, independent exact-target review and declared permissions govern local and remote effects. Scheduled and interactive publisher accounts remain distinct and correctly bound. Complete unchanged carried attention can finish green. Original-operation recovery observes before replay, preserves partial/unknown outcomes as pending, supports evidence-backed owner acknowledgement and never repeats a completed write or transfers ownership.
- [ ] AC-06: Stackweaver adopts the published installed workflow after scanner parity, independent paired review, installed/native operation checks and a successful unattended shared trial, with its local rules, credentials, commands, schedule and notifications preserved and a verified rollback path.
- [ ] AC-07: Recorded repository, package and portable-host checks pass for the exact released/installed payload. Native model/entry trials establish their actual supported surfaces. GUI, phone and unperformed surfaces remain explicitly deferred or unclaimed. Simpler installation, run/recovery and migration interfaces replace the private installer, receipt-chain and synthetic runtime machinery under the accepted tradeoffs.

## Test Plan

| Criterion | Proof |
| --- | --- |
| AC-01 | Run the canonical skill against Stackweaver's bounded fixture and a second adopter with different scanner/roots/statuses/generators. Exercise audit, selected resolve and post-merge modes, ambiguous intake, missing policy/knowledge and existing docs-impact routing. Load every shipped example through the same strict reader. Verify the eight documented helper operations accept one run directory and invoke their real stage functions. Check the canonical policy references and public guides against those operations and their actual recovery/resources. |
| AC-02 | Retain protocol/scan/snapshot tests for complete empty/findings reports, exact raw-byte Git inventories, required-unit subset/zero-input selectors, skipped/throwing units, invalid encodings/paths, unknown detectors and stale source. Omitted documents and echoed fabricated digests must fail completeness. Exercise hardened Git/filter/replace/promisor controls and a scanner with canary credentials and forbidden writes. Test still-accurate historical claims and unknown/runtime-only claims alongside confirmed stale references. |

Test before-send failure, response loss, intent/outcome interruption, partial text/labels/draft, concurrent leases, two same-directory resumers and copied-run observation-only refusals, same-account recasing, changed ID/material login, foreign recovery and stale review. Check that preflight needs no future switch outcome and that unapproved activation changes fail. ## Open Questions

### Q1: Use ordinary installed clients and record runtime versions?

**Recommendation:** A. Keep the normal plugin/client installation and record versions. Changes to plugin revision, configured role provider/command/model, source/read set, config, candidate, payload, authority or publisher require fresh review. Native client, Bun, Git and gh version strings are diagnostic when capabilities and the recorded model/config binding still match. A resume without a role process makes no new provider call or model probe.

| Option | You get | It costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A. Ordinary installed clients** | Normal client/authentication flow and a smaller installer/configuration surface. | Original executable copies are no longer privately retained. Review-bound changes need fresh review. |
| B. Retained private execution copies | The earlier exact-executable recovery environment. | Retained client/component installation and its compatibility machinery stay in scope. |

**Why this is yours:** This changes the accepted exact-runtime retention guarantee.

### Q2: Require explicit reviewed recovery after original local application evidence is lost?

**Recommendation:** A. Observe current GitHub state and require a fresh independently reviewed/approved recovery plan. The helper keeps a lost-evidence lease pending and reports its exact observed value for separately authorized manual cleanup outside the helper. It cannot infer settlement from a stopped client or repeated absence. Recovery records no invented success.

| Option | You get | It costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A. Explicit recovery after evidence loss** | Safe surviving-journal resume and acknowledgement, with a smaller remote-restoration system. | Lost local proof can require owner-reviewed manual cleanup outside the helper; automatic lease recovery is unavailable without settlement evidence. |
| B. Retain automatic remote restoration | Recovery through the earlier retained runtime and receipt chain when sufficient evidence survives. | The remote restoration and historical execution machinery remain in scope. |

**Why this is yours:** This reduces automatic recovery after loss of the original local evidence.

### Q3: Replace adoption receipt chains with a committed mode/pin gate and reviewed checklist?

**Recommendation:** A. Check staged/adopted mode, the released component fingerprint/version and a complete preflight checklist before production writes. Bind the staged candidate and activation diff before switch, and record switch results afterwards. Use the Design's fixed changed-surface table to select upgrade checks, including actual native trials for provider/model/credential/publisher changes.

| Option | You get | It costs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **A. Gated migration checklist** | Recorded migration and affected-upgrade verification with a smaller adoption interface. | The helper validates the component binding, acyclic preflight checklist and activation diff instead of walking historical receipt chains. |
| B. Retain the earlier adoption chains | The previous receipt lineage and upgrade-classification mechanism. | That framework and its tests remain part of this feature. |

**Why this is yours:** This changes the accepted adoption and upgrade evidence mechanism. Publication permission remains independently required under either option.

### R-57

NO FINDINGS

### R-60

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Design, Authority and delivery, the receipt-ref and trial-cleanup paragraphs (chunk 00290 line 13; chunk 00211 line 11); Tasks T23, "Main freshness on 2026-11-13" (chunk 01059 line 7)
EVIDENCE: Every publisher creates a retained receipt ref under `publication.receipt_ref_prefix`, "for example `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/<operation-id>`". That ref is an evidence-only commit that is never merged. "Retirement needs separately authorized cleanup after every linked proposal/recovery need is finished". The authority paragraph also grants "exact-owned trial PR/branch cleanup" for synthetic draft-PR branches that are never merged. The current pinned delivery convention at d8f07c9 says "Before deleting a stale branch, prove its current tip is merged" and "Do not delete the default branch, protected branches, active PR branches, or branches with unpublished or unmerged commits", and "Report any branch that cannot safely be removed" (delivery.md 00001 lines 12-24). Read literally, receipt retirement and trial-branch cleanup delete branches with unmerged commits. The integrated main drift also adds a stale-branch sweep to every maintenance run. It keeps "unmerged work" and reports each kept branch with its reason (workflows.md 00211 lines 1-24; authority.md 00001 row "Cleaning stale merged branches"). Retained `refs/heads/docs-drift-receipts/*` branches would therefore show up in every consumer sweep until they are retired. T23 says the new pin updates "delivery/updater/cleanup conventions" but reports only that "The applicable new page is read". It does not reconcile the updated cleanup rule with these two owned-ref deletions or with the new sweep output. The settled behavior is not wrong: the refs hold no authored work. The gap is only that the conflict with the pinned rule goes unrecorded.
PROPOSAL: Record in Context or T23, without changing the accepted normative sections, that exact-owned, evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches hold no authored work. Say that their separately granted retirement or cleanup is an intended exception to the delivery stale-branch rule, and that maintenance sweeps will list retained receipt branches as kept unmerged branches. If the owner would rather move receipts out of the `refs/heads/` example namespace, handle that through the normal revision and re-acceptance boundary.

### R-61


FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Review Log R-60 subsection (proposal on the delivery stale branch rule) and T23 freshness/integration notes
EVIDENCE: R-60 asked for one sentence in Context or T23. That sentence would say that evidence-only receipt refs and synthetic trial branches are an intended exception to the pinned delivery rule against stale unmerged branches, and that maintenance sweeps list them as kept unmerged branches. The R-60 Review Log row calls this "one cleanup-rule context nit". The current document does not record it. Context, the T23 status and integration notes, and the later T23 notes have no statement about the exception or the sweep listing. The only text on it is the R-60 record itself. The pinned delivery convention still requires stale branches to be deleted or recorded.
PROPOSAL: Add the sentence R-60 proposed to Context or the T23 notes. Alternatively, record in the Review Log that the nit was knowingly deferred and why.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/tests/vh-adlc/docs-drift/role-process.test.ts, test.each "reopened new capture rechecks native launch and actual final reply" for the removed-invocation, legacy-downgrade and invocation-cwd cases
EVIDENCE: All three new negative cases end with a bare `.toThrow()` (line 125 in the first-red log; the same call is at test 00086 line 40). readNativeRoleCapture has a distinct refusal for each fault. A schema 2 capture with no invocation fails with "modern invocation record required". A legacy capture under require_invocation fails with "modern invocation capture required". A cwd mismatch throws ArtifactChanged "docs-drift original role working directory changed". The test does not check which refusal fires, so the cases would still pass if an unrelated earlier failure triggered, such as a resealed digest or binding mismatch. The cwd fault would also pass if it lost its ArtifactChanged classification, which audit-session relies on to report scope change. role-capture.test.ts asserts exact messages for the same reader.
PROPOSAL: Give each case an expected message. Assert `.toThrow(message)` for all three and `toThrow(ArtifactChanged)` for invocation-cwd, in the style of the role-capture.test.ts table.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: shared/plugins/vh-adlc/scripts/docs-drift/audit-session.ts, reopen helper (`expected_cwd: cwd`, where `cwd = baseline.scan.checkout`); role-process.ts lines 61 and 78; role-capture.ts line 76
EVIDENCE: role-process records `invocation.cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)` and reopens with `expected_cwd: realpathSync(options.cwd)`. audit-session passes the raw `baseline.scan.checkout` string. role-capture compares the two strings exactly and throws ArtifactChanged. If scan.checkout is ever a non-canonical path to the same directory, every audit-session reopen with require_invocation fails as "role working directory changed". That would be a false scope-change refusal even though the capture is intact. The required scope does not show that scan.checkout is canonical. Only one retained session case covers this path, and the test fixtures pass canonical paths (`expected_cwd: f.repo`), so a mismatch would not show up.
PROPOSAL: Canonicalize in one place. Either pass `realpathSync(cwd)` in audit-session's reopen, or require that scan.checkout equal its realpath at the point where audit-session binds it.

### R-62

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, Audit and preparation, the feature-dialect sentence (chunk 00181 line 9). Test Plan AC-03 (chunk 00458 line 19). Tasks T23, "Upstream loop reconciliation and component111" (chunk 00995 line 5). Open Questions ("None.").

### R-63

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design, standard feature-document protection paragraph (normative-revision.patch 00052 line 3, "Reuse the existing double-star compilation in the shared matcher"); Test Plan AC-03 (patch 00063 line 2); T91 (spec 00828 line 11).
EVIDENCE: Go MatchCovers checks `strings.Contains(c, "**")` before the directory-suffix and literal branches (covers.go 00001 lines 18 to 35). It sends every such pattern, including escaped forms like `src/\**` and directory forms like `src/**/`, to compileDoubleStar. That function strips leading `./`, works on runes, gives `?` the meaning `[^/]`, and makes classes that never match `/`. Its own `coverageClass` comment says so, and the doc comment says these patterns follow the docs-impact matcher. The path.Match branch behaves differently: matching.ts runs `feature` input as bytes and lets classes consume `/` (`matches = (feature || char !== "/") && ...`, 00086 line 25), and the fixtures "class consumes slash" and "negated class consumes slash" confirm this. So simply dropping the `**` rejection while keeping `dialect === "feature"` would answer `a[^x]**` against `a/b` with true, where Go returns false. The vectors the revision requires cannot catch this. covers-cases.json has no `**` pattern whose class or `?` sits next to `/` or a multibyte rune. The existing feature fixtures test slash-consuming classes only without `**`. Neither the Design nor AC-03 says that `**` patterns take the documentation token semantics wholesale, or that `**` routing comes first and uses the raw substring.
PROPOSAL: In Design, say that any declaration containing `**` follows MatchCovers' order and uses the documentation-dialect compilation unchanged. That includes `./` normalization, rune input, and `?` and classes that exclude `/`. Other patterns keep path.Match semantics. In AC-03 and T91, add distinguishing vectors that compare the TS dialect with actual Go. Include a class and a negated class beside `/` in a `**` pattern, `?` against a multibyte rune in a `**` pattern, `src/\**`, and `src/**/` being routed before the directory-suffix check.

### R-64

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: nit
WHERE: Test Plan AC-03, the distinguishing-vector sentence (normative-revision.patch 00060 line 2, "`**/*??` against a single multibyte rune, `**/?.md` against a multibyte-rune filename"); T91 vector list (spec 00828 line 11); T91 "R63 clarification" paragraph ("distinguishing vectors explicit").
EVIDENCE: The revision gives these two vectors to show that the `**` branch uses rune input rather than the feature dialect's byte input. Neither does that as written. In matching.ts the feature dialect is already rune-aware for `?` and classes. It works out each position's decoded rune width (00001 lines 66 to 72), and the `one` token advances by that width (00086 line 14). Only `*` advances by bytes. So `**/?.md` against a filename such as `é.md` returns true on both branches, and Go's `(?:.*/)?[^/]\.md$` also returns true. It cannot distinguish the two branches. For `**/*??` the result depends on the rune's byte length. With a 2-byte rune such as `é` (c3 a9), the byte-input branch also returns false. A zero-byte star leaves `?` (width 2) and then nothing for the second `?`. A one-byte star leaves one replacement-width `?` and again nothing for the second. That equals Go's rune answer, so this vector also fails to distinguish. Only a rune of three or more bytes separates them, such as the existing fixture's `🚀` ("byte star followed by runes", `*??` true on the byte branch) or `€`. In that case the byte branch returns true and Go returns false. As written, T91 could add both vectors, see them pass with byte input kept for `**` declarations, and leave the Design's required rune input untested. No current loop or feature vector combines `**` with a multibyte path. The other listed vectors do distinguish: `a[/]**` and `a[^x]**` against `a/b` are false in Go and true through the slash-consuming feature class, and `src/**/` against a nested file distinguishes routing order. The three task-history chunks read since then (00604, 00617, 00630: T79 evidence and T80 to T90, T78) are completed historical records and do not affect the coverage revision.
PROPOSAL: In AC-03 and T91, require `**/*??` against a single rune of at least three UTF-8 bytes, for example `🚀`, with the expected Go answer false. Either drop `**/?.md` from the distinguishing list or label it a positive control. Keep the Design text unchanged, because its rune-input requirement already matches compileDoubleStar.

EVIDENCE: This revision adds a new rejection rule without defining the term it depends on or the test that applies it. Current settings have three branch namespaces under `refs/heads/`: `publication.lease_ref`, `publication.receipt_ref_prefix` and `publication.branch_prefix`. Source groups all three and rejects overlaps between them as "publication namespaces". `pr_discovery.branch_prefixes` are short, slash-terminated prefixes. The revision does not say which of these is "the publication branch namespace", so one implementer could check only `branch_prefix` (the only namespace that holds PR heads) and another could also require the lease and receipt refs to be covered. The revision also does not define "cover". It could mean an exact match, a slash-segment prefix of `branch_prefix`, or a plain string prefix, and these accept different settings. The author condition starts with "With this map present", but the namespace sentence has no such qualifier. The Owner summary and "Older single-publisher configurations keep their existing behavior" suggest the condition applies only to mapped settings. The AC-06 refusal case and T111 use the same undefined wording, so they cannot settle which implementation is correct.
PROPOSAL: Revise the sentence to read: "With this map present, at least one `pr_discovery.branch_prefixes` entry must equal `publication.branch_prefix` or be a slash-terminated prefix of it. Lease and receipt refs need no discovery coverage." If the owner wants a different relation, state that one instead. Use the same term in the Owner summary, AC-06 and T111. EVIDENCE: The revision says a case-only change in a later native login passes recovery and the publication gate, and that retained manifests and digests keep the original spelling. It also says every other payload, role and ownership binding is still checked. The gate and resume paths do not only compare a retained record with a structured identity. They rebuild the current ActionManifest and sealed plan from current native facts, then require digest or byte equality with the original plan. The reviewer capture is bound to that manifest digest. The reviewed-history verifier builds its native binding from the current PR author spelling. The actor string is part of each of these digested payloads. The Design does not say which spelling a re-derived current manifest, plan or native binding carries after a recasing.
- If it carries the newly observed spelling, the "every other payload/role binding" rule refuses at `current-manifest-changed`, at the plan inequality or at the reviewer target digest. That contradicts the required pass.
- If it carries the recorded original spelling once the case-insensitive login and exact ID match, that has to be stated. Otherwise it reads as substituting a spelling for the observation, which the paragraph forbids.
- A third implementer could remove the actor from these digest comparisons or lowercase it there. That would weaken the existing operation binding.
The positive cases in AC-06 and T112 would pass under the second and third designs, so the proof cannot tell a sound rebinding from a weakened one. This ambiguity is new in this revision's resolution of R-70.
PROPOSAL: State one rule in the second paragraph and use the same wording in the Durable reviewed publication bindings section. Example wording: "When a current manifest, plan, review target or native publication binding is re-derived for an existing operation or reviewed proposal, it carries the recorded original login spelling after the observed login matches it ignoring case and the numeric ID matches exactly. Digest and byte comparisons of those payloads stay exact, and the actor is never removed from or normalized inside a digested payload." In AC-06 and T112, add these refusal checks to the recased-observation positive:
- a re-derived plan whose actor field was lowercased or omitted
- a reviewer capture bound to a manifest digest carrying the new spelling

Then keep asserting unchanged original manifest and plan digests across the gate and resume.

Durable reviewed publication bindings, first paragraph (chunk 00350; patch 00019). Test Plan AC-06 row (chunk 00531; patch 00028: "pass history, carried delivery, post-merge closure, recovery and the publication gate", "assert the original manifest and sealed-plan digests are unchanged through gate and resume"). Tasks T112 (chunk 01065; patch 00029). Source context: manifests.ts `Bindings` (`proposals_sha256`, `delivery_sha256` sit inside the digested ActionManifest). publication-controller.ts `currentState`, which refuses when `before.worker_input_sha256 !== workerInputDigest(expected.manifest.bindings)`. `resumePublication` and the gate closure, which recollect `collectProposals` and pass the original `proposal_snapshot` and `current_proposals` to `capturePublicationInventory`. audit-publication-receipts.ts `requireInventory`, which compares `canonicalDigest(inventory.proposals)` exactly over PR rows that carry `actor`.
EVIDENCE: The new rule projects only "that operation's recorded original login" onto four named payload classes. An existing operation's digest-bound inputs also contain native PR author logins inside the proposal inventory and carried-delivery evidence. These reach the manifest through `bindings.proposals_sha256` and `bindings.delivery_sha256`, through the worker-input digest that `currentState` checks against the original plan, and through the retained `proposal_snapshot` that recovery compares with current proposals. With mapped discovery, that inventory holds PRs from both configured publishers and from legacy authors that have no recorded operation spelling. The revision leaves the following open after a case-only recasing:
- whether these inventory and delivery digests count as "re-derived current manifests" and are projected
- whether they count as "raw current observations" whose changed digest is checked under "every other source, payload ... binding" and so refuses
- which spelling applies to another publisher's or a legacy author's PR, because the projection names only the operation's own actor

One implementer would project only the manifest's top-level actor field. That implementer then refuses gate and resume when the same recased account authored an inventoried or carried proposal, because the worker-input digest changes. Another implementer would project every inventoried author that matches a recorded identity. A third would refuse only for other accounts. The AC-06 and T112 recasing positive can pass under the first or second design, depending on whether its fixture includes an open earlier proposal by the recased account. So the proof does not settle the required "carried delivery ... recovery and the publication gate" pass. This gap is new in this revision's projection rule.
PROPOSAL: In the second paragraph, and with the same wording in Durable reviewed publication bindings, state how author logins inside digest-bound proposal inventory, carried-delivery and retained proposal-snapshot inputs of an existing operation are handled after a case-only recasing. Option (a): each inventoried author is compared with that operation's original snapshot by case-insensitive login and exact numeric ID, and the re-derived input digest keeps the original snapshot spelling. Option (b): any recased inventory author changes the worker inputs, and the operation needs fresh assessment and review, with the AC-06 recovery pass limited to an unchanged inventory. In AC-06 and T112, add a resume and gate case with an open earlier proposal by the recased account and one by the other configured publisher, and state the expected outcome for each.

Exercise the same positive and refusal controls with mapped settings"). Tasks T112 (chunk 01067: "Run a pre-change scalar-record recasing control through gate/resume, closure and reviewed-history ... the same passing case in mapped settings"). The rule it should prove: Design "Publisher identity by entry", first and second paragraphs and the paragraph on proposal inventory, carried delivery and snapshots (chunk 00265: "In both scalar and mapped settings, collection uses the same case-insensitive login comparison for every explicitly declared discovery-author selector, including retained legacy-author selectors"; "Authorship, operation-actor, publication, reviewed-history eligibility, carried-delivery, receipt, closure and recovery checks compare the recorded and observed native logins case-insensitively"; "For existing operations in either scalar or mapped settings, apply the same verified projection to ... carried-delivery inputs"). The scalar compatibility paragraph (chunk 00275). Durable reviewed publication bindings, first paragraph (chunk 00346). Source context: audit-publication-receipts.ts excerpt (`proposal.actor !== settings.publication.actor` classifies the row as "Legacy or terminal proposal has no current shared reviewed delivery"). delivery-proof.ts `readAppliedDelivery` (`options.settings.publication.actor !== plan.manifest.actor` throws "delivery-policy-scope-changed"). recovery.ts `inspectRetainedOperation` (`plan.manifest.actor !== options.settings.publication.actor`).
EVIDENCE: This revision makes the native recasing rule apply in both modes. It lists carried delivery, receipts and recovery as checks that the common rule covers in scalar settings, and it makes legacy-author collection case-insensitive in scalar settings too. context.md says the same: case-only recasing "passes identity checks across gate, resume/recovery, closure, reviewed-history and carried delivery". The new scalar control covers only gate, resume, post-merge closure and reviewed-history. Three scalar surfaces are left out:
- Carried-delivery and receipt eligibility. In the current source, the scalar audit receipt collector compares the current PR author exactly with the configured scalar actor. A same-account proposal whose native login was recased is therefore classified as legacy, and no reviewed delivery is carried for it.
- Retained-operation inspection and delivery acknowledgement. `inspectRetainedOperation` and `readAppliedDelivery` also compare the configured scalar actor exactly.
- Scalar collection of a legacy-author row matched only by its author/prefix scope with no ID fallback. Only mapped discovery is proved for this case ("proving mapped discovery still collects that recased row"). The scalar control's "retained legacy rows" can still be collected through explicit IDs.

The sentence "Every later actor comparison ..." and the carried-delivery and recovery positive in AC-06 and T112 come after the entry-map requirements, and "Exercise the same positive and refusal controls with mapped settings" places those controls in mapped mode. An implementer could add the case-insensitive comparison only on the new per-entry code path and leave these scalar guards exact as "existing validation". That implementation would pass every listed scalar and mapped control while still dropping carried delivery for a recased scalar publisher and refusing its recovery inspection. This is the same divergence R-73 F-01 raised. The revision fixes the wording but leaves these surfaces out of the proof.
PROPOSAL: Extend the AC-06 scalar control and the T112 scalar control to cover all three surfaces. Pass carried delivery and audit receipt eligibility for the recased earlier same-account proposal, keeping the original `delivery_sha256` and inventory digests. Pass read-only `resume-effects` inspection and the delivery-proof scope check for the original operation. Show that scalar discovery collects a recased legacy-author row selected only by its declared author/prefix scope, with no explicit ID. Keep the changed numeric ID and material login refusals for each surface. Optionally, narrow context.md's claim until those cases exist.

Retain every original trial context/fixture/authority gate and refuse wrong IDs or production collector selection"). Tasks T111 (chunk 01067: lists trial-definition.ts but says only "Preserve case-insensitive configured-account selection and collection") and T112 (chunk 01067: "Also execute one authorized absent collector effect once despite differing configured/recorded/observed casing, preserving all original fixture/context/authority restrictions"). Source context: trial-context.ts `loadTrialContext` (excerpt lines 50 to 105) requires `canonicalJSON(manifest.identity)` to equal `TRIAL_IDENTITY` exactly. It refuses "synthetic settings identity" when `settings.publication.actor !== TRIAL_IDENTITY.actor`. trial-definition.ts `readTrialDefinition` refuses "consumer identity" when `identity.actor !== settings.publication.actor` and "candidate consumer selection" when `candidate.publication.actor !== settings.publication.actor`. It then builds `nativeIdentity` with `actor: settings.publication.actor`.
EVIDENCE: The Design names three groups of configured-versus-recorded guards that become case-insensitive: publication shape/gates, retained inspection and delivery-proof scope. It also requires the collector transport to keep its "original context ... gates" unchanged, and AC-06 says to "Retain every original trial context/fixture/authority gate". The trial setup readers that feed the collector and the hosted trial have their own exact actor guards, and the spec puts them in neither group. The synthetic context gate requires the configured scalar actor to equal the fixed fixture identity byte for byte. While that gate stays exact, the configured spelling cannot differ in case from the fixture's recorded identity. So the AC-06 and T112 collector case, which requires "configured/recorded/observed login casing differing at the same fixture account ID", cannot be built while every original trial context gate is kept. trial-definition.ts also refuses a configured actor whose casing differs from the definition identity. Its result records the configured spelling as the native identity. Once that refusal is relaxed, this becomes a substitution the Design forbids ("Never substitute a configured spelling for an observed identity"). Implementers will diverge:
- One relaxes these trial-context and trial-definition comparisons as configured-login validations.
- Another keeps them as retained context gates, then either cannot build the collector case or rebuilds the fixture identity to match.
- A third relaxes the definition check but keeps the configured spelling in `nativeIdentity`.
The proof does not say which is correct. This traces to the current revision, which added the collector-transport rule and the collector absent-effect case.
PROPOSAL: In the second paragraph, say how the trial setup identity comparisons are classified. Example wording: "The synthetic trial-context and trial-definition comparisons between configured `publication.actor` and the recorded fixture or definition identity are configured-login validations under the same case-insensitive rule, with exact numeric IDs. The resulting trial identity keeps the recorded or observed native spelling, never the configured one. Fixture-ID, namespace, authority and evidence-kind gates stay exact." Use the same classification in the AC-06 sentence "Retain every original trial context/fixture/authority gate". Name trial-context.ts and trial-definition.ts in T111 or T112. Add a refusal for a trial-definition native identity that carries the configured spelling.

EVIDENCE: This revision puts the candidate-versus-current selector comparison in the first table row. That row's required check is to "verify the authoritative numeric ID at the native or recorded boundary" and to "Return the recorded or observed native spelling". Both sides of the candidate-versus-current comparison are configured strings from two settings blobs, so neither carries a numeric ID or a native spelling. The row's check therefore cannot be applied as written. The spec also leaves open how the case-insensitive result relates to the protected-setting rules. `publication` is a protected setting, a candidate whose actor differs only in case still changes the protected bytes, and AC-06 requires fresh affected-entry evidence for an identity-map change. The spec does not say whether a case-only candidate actor change, or a case-only change to a candidate `entry_actors` value in mapped mode, counts as the same selector or as an identity change. The proof does not settle it either. AC-06 and T111 add controls only for trial-context and trial-definition configured actors measured against the recorded fixture or definition identity. No control and no task text covers a candidate whose actor differs from current settings only in case. Two implementations would both pass every listed control: one keeps today's exact `candidate.publication.actor !== settings.publication.actor` refusal, which contradicts the table, and the other relaxes it. The relaxed version could also accept the change silently with no affected-entry trial. This traces to the current revision, which added the table row in response to R-75 F-01.
PROPOSAL: Give candidate-versus-current selectors their own rule outside the native/recorded row. For example: "A candidate and current configured actor selector compare ignoring case for trial and candidate selection, with no numeric-ID or spelling result. It should pass candidate consumer selection with the stated upgrade classification. Keep a refusal for a candidate login that changes beyond casing. Update context.md to use the same wording.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: docs/specs/shared-docs-drift.md, Design "Publisher identity by entry", Stackweaver paragraph (line 282: "configure `hosted` as `github-actions[bot]`", "The observed actor is the PR author for reviewed-history reads and the authenticated publisher for that operation's own gate and resume", "A login change beyond casing or a different numeric ID retains the existing changed-account refusal"), and the identity-comparison table; Context T16 paragraph ("the authenticated actor query was checked against GitHub's installation-token example and User viewer/database-ID schema"); Tasks T16 evidence (line 601: "Native actor identity now uses the fixed authenticated viewer query documented for installation tokens"); Test Plan AC-05 and AC-06 rows; Tasks T111 and T112. Source context: publish.ts `verifyPublicationGate` (`identity.actor !== plan.manifest.actor`, with identity from `transport.identity()`); report-history.ts `readReviewedProposal` (`native.actor` from the REST pull `current.actor`); collector-proof.ts excerpt line 8 (fixture PR `user: { login: identity.actor, type: "Bot" }`).
EVIDENCE: The hosted entry is a GitHub App bot. Under the Design, its identity is compared across two native sources. The authenticated publisher comes from the GraphQL viewer query that T16 selected, and PR authorship for history, carried delivery and closure comes from REST pull metadata. The common rule tolerates only casing differences and refuses any other login change. GitHub's GraphQL `Bot` actor `login` is the app slug without the `[bot]` suffix (the form the GitHub CLI renders as `app/<slug>`), while REST `user.login` carries `github-actions[bot]`. This packet has no observation of what the installation-token `viewer` query returns for `GITHUB_TOKEN`. The Design neither states how bot login forms from different endpoints are reconciled nor requires them to come from one representation. Every local fixture builds the PR author and the publisher identity from the same `identity.actor` string, so no listed AC-05 or AC-06 control can tell a correct implementation from one that refuses the real bot. If the forms differ, the configured `hosted: github-actions[bot]` fails configured-versus-native validation, and the bot's own reviewed proposals read as a material login change. That would surface only in the separately authorized paid hosted trial, after T111 to T113 are built. This traces to the accepted publisher-by-entry revision, not to the current drift.
PROPOSAL: Raise an Open Question, or state explicitly, how a bot actor's login is represented and compared when the viewer and REST forms differ. For example, compare on the authoritative numeric ID with one canonical REST login form, and never strip suffixes heuristically. Add a T111/T112 local control in which the hosted publisher's authenticated identity and its REST PR author use the two documented bot forms at the same numeric ID, with the stated expected outcome.

The previous summary stated three things the new one drops. First, corrections receive independent review before publication. Second, a consolidation closes its originals only after merge and fresh per-hunk checks. Third, mapped settings require both publisher accounts in explicit discovery authors and discovery coverage of `publication.branch_prefix`, while existing scalar settings keep their current validation. The new text also no longer says what the shared workflow does: it checks scanner findings and existing PRs against current source, reports, and prepares selected corrections. The mapped discovery requirement is a rejection default an adopter meets directly (Design line 280). The review and closure gates are the feature's main permission limits. The detailed sections keep all of these rules, so nothing an implementer builds changes.
PROPOSAL: Restore one or two sentences naming the workflow's audit and resolve behavior, independent review before publication, merge-first closure, and the mapped-only discovery-author and branch-prefix requirement with scalar validation unchanged.


### R-79
