T111, T112, T32, T33 and T38 now use that route", "Development review routing supplies no provider-operation result"); Owner summary, fifth paragraph (spec 00002 line 18: "Development reviews use Claude Code Opus 5.5. Installed-entry runtime trials retain their selected providers"); Tasks T33 (spec 00152 line 11: "before sending it to Claude Code Opus 5.5") and T37 (spec 00180 line 3: "Review C, verify record-only D"); Design "Protected adoption and runtime gate", `adopted` paragraph (spec 00034 line 7: "recorded passing-trial and paired-review verdicts/identities/digests, exact current component/worker/reviewer/publisher bindings"); Design cutover-evidence paragraph (spec 00039 line 5: "Obtain paired source-candidate review at C ... Retain its native returned records") and first post-cutover runtime gate (spec 00040 line 3: "paired source-candidate acceptance"); Design "Upgrades after adoption" (spec 00050 line 5: "Obtain paired source/compatibility acceptance at C"); Design "Migration and rollback" (spec 00053 line 3: "Fresh paired review binds the exact installed shared payload and consumer candidate"); Test Plan AC-06 ("Verify non-circular C review then D record-only assembly") and AC-07 ("Obtain exact-payload paired acceptance before migration"); T21 "Compatible successor progress" ("require fresh paired review and three install receipts at the new C/component"). Source context: adoption.ts `verifyRecordedReceipts` (source-excerpts/adoption.ts/0125 00002 lines 34 to 44). context.md ("This changes no protected consumer role config").
EVIDENCE: This revision's response to R-78 F-01 puts "paired reviews" and T33 on the Claude Code development-review route. It also says that route "supplies no provider-operation result". The paired source-candidate review at C is not only a development review, though. It becomes a required `paired_review` receipt in the portable adoption attestation that the runtime gate checks. In the implemented gate, adoption.ts maps `name === "paired_review"` to the `copilot` execution entry. It requires `receipt.reviewer_model` to match `bindings.settings.execution.entries.copilot.reviewer.model` with an identity source, and requires `client_bindings` to equal that entry's reviewer client. So the gate accepts paired-review evidence only from the consumer's protected Copilot-entry reviewer role. The Design also binds paired-review "identities" and reviewer bindings into the attestation. After this revision the document gives two incompatible readings. (1) T33's Claude Code review is the C paired review. Its receipt would then fail the gate's reviewer-model and client-binding checks, unless someone changes the protected reviewer binding, which context.md says does not happen. (2) T33 is an extra development review. Then no task produces the operation-bound `paired_review` receipt that D's attestation needs, and T37 does not name a reviewer either. One implementer would run only the Claude Code T33 review and relax or bypass the receipt binding. Another would add a separate Copilot-entry paired review. A per-criterion review of AC-06 and AC-07 could rule either way. Before this revision, the requested development reviewer and the consumer's reviewer role were both Copilot Opus 5.5, so T33's wording was harmless. The divergence traces to this revision's routing paragraph and the T33 edit.
PROPOSAL: State in Context which review produces the attestation's `paired_review` receipt. If it is the consumer's protected Copilot-entry reviewer role, as the accepted Design and adoption.ts imply, say so in T33 and T37, and keep the Claude Code review in T33 as an additional development review that supplies no receipt. If the owner wants Claude Code to supply that receipt, raise an Open Question. That choice changes the protected reviewer binding and needs the normal revision and re-acceptance route.

Under the revised Design, no Copilot-model review takes place at all. The standard says an implementation review returns one record per criterion with its exact accepted text, and its verdict rests on executed evidence for that text. One reviewer could read "Copilot model ... reviews" literally and mark AC-07 untested or not met. Another could read it as Copilot-entry reviews now performed by Claude and mark it met. The proof row and the criterion now say different things. Context also says Acceptance Criteria are unchanged, which reads as if the criterion text was deliberately kept. Context "Claude review follow-up and native identity acquisition", first paragraph (spec 00006 line 28: "The owner's 2026-11-14 instruction selects Claude Code Opus 5.5 for feature-development spec, scoped source, implementation and paired reviews", "The owner's request for Claude Code reviews applies to that review too. Owner summary, fifth paragraph (spec 00002 line 18). Tasks T113 (spec 00149 line 2: "Update the staged candidate reviewer bindings to pinned Claude Code Opus 5.5 for all entries"). Open Questions (spec 00180 line 9: "None."). Source context: settings.ts `executionSettings` (separate `hosted`, `copilot` and `claude` entries, each with its own reviewer client); adoption.ts `verifyRecordedReceipts` (only `paired_review` maps to the `copilot` entry reviewer; `hosted_advisory` binds the hosted reviewer model and client).

It proposed an Open Question if the owner wanted Claude Code to supply that receipt. This revision instead infers that the request "applies to that review too". It then goes further and changes every entry's runtime reviewer, including the hosted scheduled reviewer and the Claude-entry reviewer. Neither of those produces the paired receipt or a development review. That changes production behavior: every scheduled and interactive real-corpus publication and closure review would run on Claude Code. That brings a Claude reviewer credential into the hosted job, Claude usage on every scheduled run, and fresh trials for all three entries. The document already records a Claude Code review refused by its monthly spend limit, which would make scheduled runs red under the status policy. T113 names "credential inputs" without saying what they are. ### R-86

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph ("Preserve the caller's files, index, refs and Git objects"); Design "User workflow and local configuration", second paragraph (entry point `snapshot.ts:248`); Test Plan AC-03 ("must preserve files/index/refs/objects"); Tasks T124.
EVIDENCE: This finding comes from the declared architecture proportionality lens. T124 carries the existing caller-state mechanism forward, but the Design does not say what the check observes or how often it runs. At the target, `captureRepositoryState` (snapshot.ts:248 to 266) hashes four things: the raw index file (`fileDigest(gitPath(repo, "--git-path", "index"))` at line 258), a Git-directory inventory that excludes objects, the common directory and an object-directory inventory. runScan compares this capture before and after collection and reports "caller-changed" (scan.ts:274). Some events change those digests even though docs-drift writes nothing: a stat-only index refresh, such as the one `git status` writes, or automatic maintenance in the caller's repository. A correct run then reports incomplete. T21 already saw this kind of churn ("transient Git metadata churn ... detached maintenance launches from fixture commits"). It was fixed only by disabling maintenance in owned fixtures, and that fix cannot be applied to a consumer checkout. The archived architecture diagnostic names the same cost in its finding 3. It recommends one check per invocation, limited to HEAD, index, refs and tracked content. A shared primitive already compares at content level: `snapshot` in scripts/loop/review-route.ts:73 to 100 hashes HEAD, refs, `ls-files --stage` index entries, status and file contents rather than raw index bytes. The Design does not reuse it and does not explain why it cannot preserve the outcome. The bodies of directoryInventory and objectInventory were not supplied, so whether those inventories include timestamps is not verified here. This traces to the current revision, which rewrote the Design and kept this subsystem.
PROPOSAL: State the observable caller-preservation rule: one capture per invocation comparing HEAD, refs, index entries and tracked/untracked content, with stat-only index rewrites and maintenance metadata treated as unchanged. If that is not possible, explain why the shared review-route snapshot cannot be reused. Add an AC-03 Test Plan control in which a caller index stat refresh and a background `git maintenance run` during collection leave the run complete, next to the existing real-write refusal.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", third paragraph ("Reuse the existing bounded executor in `scripts/triage/owned-process.ts:60` for native role processes ... add only those needed options") and fourth paragraph ("Reuse compatible execution and identity primitives from `scripts/loop/review-route.ts:204`"); Context "Simplification basis on 2026-11-15"; Tasks T123 ("Remove the duplicate executor after parity"); Test Plan AC-07.
EVIDENCE: The docs-drift executor has callers other than role processes, and its contract covers more than output limits and cancellation. runScan calls `executeOwnedProcess` with `watched_outputs: [output]` (scan.ts:252), which bounds the scanner's `--protocol-json` file while it is being written. The executor also offers `capture: "memory"` with bounded in-memory drains and a capture deadline (docs-drift/owned-process.ts:24, 29, 58 to 66). T80 to T85 added this so native mutation output never stores raw response bytes on disk. It also supports a `started` callback that returns false to cancel startup capture (line 80). triage `runOwnedClient` writes stdout and stderr only to files (triage/owned-process.ts:67 to 68) and has no watched-output or memory-capture option. T123 removes the duplicate executor, which forces the scanner and the native transport onto the triage executor. Yet the Design lists only "output-limit and cancellation controls" as options to add. One implementer adds those two options and moves native mutations to file capture. That loses the T80 privacy property and breaks the Design's own rule to "Keep credentials and raw secret-bearing environments out of artifacts". Another keeps the docs-drift executor for those callers and cannot satisfy T123. There is a second gap. `runReviewRoute` returns pending whenever the client did not run in a PID namespace (review-route.ts:236). Docs-drift role execution today accepts the marker boundary, and the archived architecture diagnostic capture itself records `"boundary": "marker"` with confirmed cleanup (architecture-validation.json). The Design does not say whether docs-drift reviews inherit that mandatory boundary. If they do, every review stays pending on macOS interactive hosts, which AC-07 still requires. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Name every option the shared executor must gain: watched output files, bounded memory capture with its deadline, startup-callback cancellation, output limit and cancellation. Name the callers that move to it. Alternatively, state that the scanner and native transport keep a separate executor, and drop "remove the duplicate executor". State whether docs-drift role reviews require the PID-namespace boundary or accept confirmed marker cleanup. Add T123/AC-07 controls showing that scanner watched-output bounds still hold and that native mutation output never reaches disk after the swap.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Context "Owner summary", third paragraph ("with fresh review when changed execution inputs affect a pending run"); Design "Installation and role execution", first and second paragraphs; Q1 option A ("Client/plugin changes can require fresh review for a pending run"); Test Plan AC-07 ("requiring fresh review where specified"); Tasks T122 ("changed execution inputs ... controls").
EVIDENCE: The run records "plugin revision, native client version, Bun version, requested model, observed model and operation target", and "Resuming with changed execution inputs requires a fresh independent review". The document never says which recorded fields count as execution inputs. It drops "an exact Bun-version equality check for every effect", but does not say whether a Bun, gh, Git or client patch release between an interrupted publish and its resume is a changed input. The summary's "affect" and Q1's "can require" leave that decision to the implementer. The AC-07 proof tests fresh review "where specified", but nothing specifies it. Three implementations are possible, and all pass the same proof. One treats any recorded version difference as a change. An unattended scheduled resume after a hosted runner image update then always goes pending for review, which repeats the cost the architecture diagnostic attributes to the exact Bun check (its finding 8). Another compares only the plugin revision and the role client and model. A third compares nothing. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: List the recorded fields whose change requires fresh review on resume, for example plugin revision, role client identity and observed model. List the fields recorded only for diagnosis, for example Bun, Git and gh patch versions. Use the same list in the summary and in Q1. Make the AC-07 and T122 controls change one field of each kind and state the expected outcome.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", first, second, fourth and sixth paragraphs; Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", first paragraph; Context "Owner summary", third paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 ("concurrent leases"); Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: The reviewer contract asks what happens under concurrent requests and partial failure. Two retained outcomes now have no defined path. (1) Concurrency within one run. The lease is repository-scoped and records the run as its owner, so two local resumers of the same run directory both satisfy it. The Design requires recording intent before sending and observing before replay. T44 showed that marker observation alone still allows "duplicate creation from concurrent resumers". The old design closed that gap with journal revision-CAS attempt claims made immediately before dispatch. The new Design does not say how two invocations on one run directory exclude each other, and the AC-05 proof names only "concurrent leases". (2) A lost run directory while the lease is held. The Design says "Release only the run's own lease ... Never infer ownership from branch or resource naming" and "A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes". When loss of the local journal removes the proof of ownership, the freshly reviewed replacement plan sees an unknown owner and cannot write, and nothing can release the old lease. The old design had separately authorized exact-owned lease cleanup (T43, T46, and the authority.md "Owned lease release" row). The new text retires reconstruction but gives no replacement. Implementers will diverge between a permanent block, a new owner-approved lease release and inferred ownership. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State the exclusion rule within one run, for example an atomic claim in the run directory before each dispatch. Add a two-resumer control to AC-05 and T125 that counts each effect once. State how a lease is released when its run directory is lost. For example: only with the owner's current approval of the exact observed lease object, after observation shows no unsettled effect, recorded as non-success. Add a matching AC-05/AC-07 control.

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Scope, third paragraph; Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", second, third and fifth paragraphs; Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", third paragraph ("Before each write, recheck ..."); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph ("Two guarantees become more practical"); Open Questions Q1.
EVIDENCE: The current runtime refuses production effects unless the committed adoption is verified. `selectApprovedRuntime` calls `verifyRuntimeAdoption` (bootstrap.ts:465, adoption.ts:602). parseSettings requires a component pin when `publication.mode` is adopted (settings.ts:334). T64 makes the runtime "deny staged production effects". Scope and Design replace "C/D adoption receipt assembly and affected-entry upgrade classification with one recorded migration checklist", and say future upgrades receive "the checks affected by that change". The pre-write recheck list (permission, identity, freshness, preimage, review/payload) no longer mentions adoption or the staged/adopted mode. No sentence says what stops a staged configuration from publishing in production, or who decides which checks an upgrade affects. One implementer keeps a committed mode gate. Another removes it along with the receipt chain and relies on the checklist. This is also a third reduction of an accepted guarantee: machine-enforced adoption, plus fresh affected-entry trial evidence for provider, identity or protected changes. The summary and Q1 present only two reductions. Q1 also bundles two separable guarantees into one choice, so the owner cannot accept ordinary client management while keeping remote restoration, or the reverse. Q1's recommendation also does not use the template's `**Recommendation:** A.` form. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State the runtime gate that remains for production effects, for example a committed adopted mode plus the recorded checklist reference, checked before every write. State how the checks affected by an upgrade are determined. Raise the replacement of adoption receipts and upgrade classification as its own Open Question. Split Q1 into one question on installed-client management and one on recovery after lost evidence, each in template form. Update the summary to name every guarantee that is reduced.

FINDING: F-07
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Resolution, feedback and reports", third paragraph ("Use maintained native archive handling instead of shipping a second vendored ZIP implementation solely for history"; "Enforce configured count/age/byte bounds"); Test Plan AC-05 ("incomplete history"); Tasks T126.
EVIDENCE: Actions artifacts are ZIP archives. The document's own Sources say Bun's archive API provides "tar extraction". T10 chose a bounded vendored reader so that "A copied bundle runs without node_modules, fetch or system unzip". It tested traversing and duplicate names, nonregular entries, false sizes and expansion bounds. The revision does not say what "maintained native archive handling" means, and each obvious candidate has a gap. `gh run download` extracts to disk without an expansion bound before extraction. System `unzip` is a new host dependency that the ordinary installation does not provide. Neither enforces `max_entry_bytes` before bytes are written. T126 removes the vendored path "after replacement coverage", but the proof names only changed, foreign and incomplete report controls. No traversal, duplicate-entry or expansion-bound control remains in the Test Plan. One implementer keeps equivalent bounds; another relies on gh extraction and checks sizes afterwards. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Name the archive mechanism and the host dependency it adds. State that entry count, per-entry and total expanded bytes, path traversal, duplicate names and nonregular entries are refused before extraction, or inside a private bounded directory. Add those malicious-archive controls to the AC-05 row and to T126 before the vendored reader is removed.

FINDING: F-08
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", seventh paragraph ("Publisher selection supports the scheduled bot and interactive owner. Compare configured/native login case-insensitively with exact numeric IDs"); Design "User workflow and local configuration", third paragraph ("publisher accounts"); Test Plan AC-05; Tasks T122 and T125.
EVIDENCE: The rewritten Design condenses the accepted identity rules into two sentences, and the new Test Plan drops two controls that earlier reviews made required. First, R-78 F-02 showed that the hosted bot appears as `github-actions[bot]` in REST and as the bare slug in GraphQL, at the same numeric ID. Context "Native authenticated publisher acquisition" records T111's fix: resolve the viewer's numeric ID through REST `user/{id}` and keep the REST login. Second, R-68 F-02 and R-69 F-01 required that mapped publisher logins appear in `pr_discovery.authors` and that a discovery prefix covers `publication.branch_prefix`. settings.ts enforces both at lines 340 to 346. T122 replaces the settings schema and T125 consolidates publication. Yet the AC-05 proof names only "both bot/owner accounts", recasing and changed ID or material login. A replacement could compare the GraphQL viewer login with REST PR authors, or drop the discovery-author relation. It would then refuse the real bot or silently miss the owner's proposals, while every listed control passes with fixtures that use one string for both forms. The lens forbids assuming these stronger invariants may be silently dropped. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: State in the publisher paragraph that the publisher account is acquired by numeric ID with the REST login kept, and that each configured publisher login must be a discovery author with a covering branch prefix. Add the dual bot representation control and the missing-discovery-author refusal to the AC-05 row and to T122/T125.

### R-87

FINDING: F-01
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Review Log, the R-86 row and the sentence before it (spec 0066 lines 13 to 16; R86-corrections.patch 0015 lines 15 to 19).
EVIDENCE: The table ends with the R-85 row, then a blank line, then the paragraph "Historical returned review records are preserved verbatim ... Records for R-57 and R-60 onward appear below." The R-86 row is appended directly under that paragraph. A Markdown table needs contiguous rows below its header and delimiter. A `|` line that follows a paragraph line continues that paragraph, so R-86 is no longer a row of the Review Log table. A reader of the rendered page, or a parser that reads the table as one contiguous block, would treat R-85 as the latest spec review. The R-87 row and any acceptance row that follow would land in the same place. The standard's `review_log_columns` rule and the `spec_review_fresh`, `spec_review_clean` and `accepted` conditions all depend on reading that table. I could not run the binary, so I have not observed whether its parser tolerates the gap. This traces to the current revision's append of the R-86 row.
PROPOSAL: Move the R-86 row up so it directly follows the R-85 row inside the table, and keep the historical-records sentence after the table. Run `vh-adlc review check --review R-86` and `vh-adlc next` to confirm the binary reports R-86 as the latest spec review.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", third paragraph ("Move scanner, native GitHub mutation and role-process callers together") and fourth paragraph ("Docs-drift accepts its existing confirmed marker-based process cleanup on portable hosts and records that boundary honestly. It gains no stronger confinement claim from sharing an executor"); Tasks T123 ("portable marker-cleanup support"); Test Plan AC-07.
EVIDENCE: The docs-drift executor has no marker-based cleanup and no cleanup confirmation. `executeOwnedProcess` (docs-drift/owned-process.ts:22 to 123) signals only the process group (lines 31 to 33 and 92). It sets no owner marker and returns no `confirmed`, `survivors` or `boundary` field (`OwnedProcess`, lines 3 to 6). Role evidence carries that type (manifests.ts:4 and 10). `verifyRoleIdentity` checks only exit code, reaping, timeout, cancellation and output limit (manifests.ts:247). Marker cleanup with `confirmed` exists only in triage `runOwnedClient` (triage/owned-process.ts:29 to 49 and 101 to 107). The archived `"boundary": "marker"` record (architecture-validation.json) describes the reviewer harness, not docs-drift role execution. R-86 F-02 asserted "Docs-drift role execution today accepts the marker boundary" without verifying it, and this revision adopted that wording. So "existing" is false, and the Design does not say whether a docs-drift role or scanner result must now require `confirmed` true and zero survivors. One implementer adds that requirement; another keeps today's exit/reap check, and both cite the text. The caller list is also incomplete. T11 extracted this executor so that "fixed-ref authenticated fetches retain its tested group cancellation" (proposals.ts and git-read.ts in its file list), and T12 lists it for history retrieval. Neither caller group is named among the callers that move before the duplicate is removed.
PROPOSAL: Say that docs-drift role and scanner results gain marker-based cleanup from the shared executor and must report confirmed cleanup with no survivors, or state that they keep today's group-only check. Name every current caller of the docs-drift executor, including the proposal fetch and history paths, in the Design and T123.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph ("Preserve the caller's logical state, including HEAD, its branch/owned refs, staged index entries, tracked/untracked work and configuration. Reuse the shared logical snapshot"); Test Plan AC-03; Tasks T124.
EVIDENCE: This finding comes from the declared architecture proportionality lens. The only shared logical snapshot is `snapshot` in scripts/loop/review-route.ts:73 to 100. It returns head, head_ref, refs, index, status, ignored and contents. It reads no Git configuration, so it cannot preserve the "configuration" the Design lists. The old capture covered config only through its raw Git-metadata inventory (snapshot.ts:248 to 266), which this revision drops as non-semantic. The shared snapshot also hashes the contents of every ignored file and every ignored directory tree (review-route.ts:78 and 85 to 88). The Design lists only tracked and untracked work. In an active consumer checkout, ignored build or dependency trees change whenever the caller's own tools write to them. Hashing them would recreate the false "caller-changed" failures and the cost that the archived diagnostic's finding 3 recommends removing ("narrowed to HEAD, index, refs and tracked content"). One implementer reuses the snapshot unchanged and inherits both problems. Another adds a config check and drops ignored content. The AC-03 controls (index stat refresh, git maintenance) pass either way.
PROPOSAL: State whether ignored content is part of caller preservation, and how configuration is observed: extend the shared snapshot with a scoped option, or add a separate config digest. Add an AC-03 control in which an ignored build file changes during collection, with the expected outcome stated.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph ("The lease and atomic journal claim identify the active writer instance as well as the run. Acquire a revision-CAS attempt claim immediately before dispatch. A second resumer, including a copied run on another host, cannot dispatch while that claim is active or unsettled. Reuse the existing attempt/claim safeguards"); Test Plan AC-05 ("two same-directory resumers and copied-run cross-host resumers"); Tasks T125 ("concurrent/cross-host same-run claims").
EVIDENCE: A revision-CAS attempt claim lives in the run directory's journal. A run directory copied to another host carries its own copy of that journal, so a claim written on host A is invisible on host B. The existing safeguards do not solve this case. T46 records "a random run nonce before acquisition" in the original journal, and a byte copy carries that nonce, so the copy can show it owns the lease. T44 handled mirrors only through the explicit restore path, which writes an observation-only header; a plain copy has no such header. The Design does not say how "the active writer instance" differs from the run identity once the directory is copied. It also does not say how a legitimate resumer on the original host (a new process, and so a new instance) takes over from a crashed instance while a copy cannot. Implementers would diverge: a remote per-attempt claim ref; a lease CAS handover to each new instance, which races a live original writer between its recheck and dispatch; or no detection. The AC-05 copied-run control cannot pass on the mechanism as written. This traces to the current revision's response to R-86 F-04, which asked only for same-directory exclusion.
PROPOSAL: Define the cross-host exclusion mechanism: what instance identity the remote lease records, how a resumer takes it over by compare-and-swap, and what stops a live original writer from dispatching after a takeover. Alternatively, limit the claim to same-directory resumers and make copied runs observation-only until a takeover the owner approves.

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", sixth paragraph ("only after the original writer is confirmed stopped and no mutation remains unsettled ... Missing ownership, live-writer or request-settlement evidence keeps the lease pending"); Q2 recommendation ("a stopped original writer and settled mutations"); Owner summary, third paragraph; Test Plan AC-05 ("Test exact-owner lease abandonment after journal loss with a stopped writer and settled requests"); Tasks T125.
EVIDENCE: Lease cleanup after journal loss requires proof that no mutation remains unsettled. The attempt and settlement records that would supply that proof are in the lost journal. The document's own history says remote observation alone cannot settle a request. T48: "two absence reads beyond a client timeout cannot establish that an accepted remote request will never complete". T50: "A delayed accepted create-only request can reacquire the nonce". The Design names no other settlement evidence, such as the writer's process record, an elapsed bound or owner attestation. Read literally, the precondition can never hold after real journal loss, which brings back the permanent lease block R-86 F-04 raised. Read loosely, an implementer may accept "writer stopped plus no visible effect", which the document itself says is insufficient. Neither the Design nor the AC-05 proof says who confirms the writer has stopped, or how. A fixture can satisfy "settled requests" only by inventing evidence the scenario lacks.
PROPOSAL: State which evidence can establish settlement and a stopped writer after journal loss, for example owner attestation plus a stated time bound plus a fresh observation, and accept the T48 risk explicitly. Otherwise state that the lease stays blocked until the owner performs manual cleanup outside the helper. Raise the choice in Q2 and put that exact evidence into the AC-05 control.

FINDING: F-06
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph ("Before every production write, adopted mode, the installed revision's pin match and a complete checklist bound to the consumer/config/plugin must pass ... It records ... reviewed consumer mapping, scheduled trial, legacy inventory/freeze, routing switch and rollback result"); Context "Terms used here", Adoption and Migration checklist rows; Q3; Test Plan AC-07; Tasks T128 and T130.
EVIDENCE: Adoption is "a reviewed configuration switch from staged to adopted mode, bound to the approved plugin pin and checklist". The checklist is "bound to the consumer/config/plugin" and must already contain the "routing switch" result. If the adopted config binds the checklist and the checklist binds the config, the digests form a cycle. The routing-switch result cannot exist before the adoption commit that performs or enables the switch. The retired design broke this cycle with the reviewed source candidate C and the record-only child D (historical R-79 F-01, R-80 F-04 and T56: "the exact direct-child record-only cutover shape"). The revision removes C/D assembly without a replacement. It also does not say where the checklist lives (committed in the consumer, external, or in the run directory), which config bytes it binds (staged candidate or adopted), or how the gate checks that the adopted config differs from the bound one only by mode and reference. Implementers will build incompatible gates, and the AC-07 "incomplete/mismatched checklist" control cannot tell them apart.
PROPOSAL: Define the checklist's storage location and binding order: for example, it binds the reviewed staged config and plugin pin, and the adoption commit changes only the mode and the checklist reference, which the gate verifies. Say when the routing-switch and rollback rows are recorded relative to the first production write.

FINDING: F-07
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph ("exact approved plugin pin", "the installed revision's pin match"); Design "Installation and role execution", first and second paragraphs ("Record the actual plugin revision", "Changes to plugin revision"); Q1; Q3 recommendation ("the approved plugin pin"); Owner summary, third paragraph; Test Plan AC-07 ("wrong installed pin").
EVIDENCE: The gate compares an "approved plugin pin" with "the installed revision", but the document never says what value the pin holds or how an ordinarily installed helper observes its own revision. The retained observation of ordinary installation shows `codex plugin add` reporting only version `0.22.0` and a cache path (stored R-83 F-02; Context "Owner decision and publication authority"). The whole-payload source identity that bootstrap.ts computed (T59) belongs to the retired retained-runtime machinery. In this repository "plugin pin" already names the vhco-pro/docs rules revision passed as `--plugin-pin` (for example `vh-adlc lint ... --plugin-pin 1ccf8f31...` in Context, and the reviewer dispatch contract). One implementer compares version strings, and unreleased candidates can share those. Another compares a commit SHA that the installed payload may not expose. A third hashes the installed payload. Each passes a "wrong installed pin" control built for its own representation.
PROPOSAL: Define the pin value (released version, commit or payload digest) and the mechanism the installed helper uses to observe it, and use a term distinct from the existing `--plugin-pin` docs revision. Make the AC-07 control change that exact value.

WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph ("Its real-corpus trial exception requires a current exact grant for the selected consumer/source, native entry/publisher, trial run and bounded advisory draft effects ... FINDING: F-11
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", fourth paragraph ("The preliminary paired shared/consumer migration review runs through actual Claude Code Opus 5.5 under the exact private packet approval"); Acceptance Criteria AC-06 ("independent paired review"); Owner summary, fourth paragraph; Test Plan AC-06; Tasks T129 ("preliminary paired migration reviews") and T130 ("paired migration").
EVIDENCE: The only paired review the Design names is "preliminary". T129 runs it before release, before the marketplace install and before the real-corpus trial. T130 says only "verify ... paired migration". "Preliminary" implies a final review, but no task or Design sentence names one, and nothing says whether the released payload and the final consumer mapping must be reviewed again. The retired design reviewed the final C at the released pin and installed payload after the freeze (historical R-79 F-01, R-80 F-04, T37). FINDING: F-12
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", second paragraph ("Native client, Bun, Git and gh version strings are diagnostic only when setup capability checks and the observed model still match"); Owner summary, third paragraph; Q1 recommendation; Test Plan AC-07 ("A diagnostic-only Bun/Git/gh/native-client version patch keeps unchanged review usable when capabilities and observed model match"); Tasks T122.
EVIDENCE: Resuming a publication or closure runs no worker or reviewer process, so it produces no new native observation of the model. The rule makes a client version change diagnostic only when "the observed model still match[es]", but does not say what is compared at resume. One implementer compares the configured requested model with the originally observed model and treats a client patch as diagnostic without invoking anything. Another requires a fresh model probe or role invocation to observe the model, which adds a provider call and credential use to every unattended resume after a runner image update. ### R-88

FINDING: F-01
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph (host stamp, same-host resume, Actions concurrency; spec 0013 line 17) and sixth paragraph (local journal loss; spec 0013 line 25 to 0014 line 1); Design "One run directory and a smaller helper interface", first paragraph (spec 0013 line 7); Open Questions Q2 (spec 0064 lines 28 to 37); Context "Owner summary", third paragraph (spec 0001 line 79); Test Plan AC-05 (spec 0016 line 23); Tasks T125 (spec 0017 line 12).
EVIDENCE: The Design records "its origin host stamp and canonical directory", says "Normal resume requires the same host and directory", and says "A copied run or another host gets observation-only inspection and no automatic lease takeover". After loss of local records, "The helper does not clear a lease" and hands off to "manual cleanup outside the helper". The run directory is a "private external run directory", and nothing says where the scheduled hosted entry keeps it. GitHub-hosted jobs run on a fresh runner instance, so the hosted run directory disappears when the job ends, and any later job is "another host". So any scheduled publication interrupted after lease acquisition can never be resumed. Causes include a timeout, runner loss, or a cancellation by the Actions concurrency that "supplements that lease". The lease stays held, and "A conflicting/unknown owner blocks writes" stops every later scheduled publication until the owner cleans up by hand. For the scheduled bot, which is the main production path, journal loss is the normal failure outcome rather than an exception. Q2's cost column ("Lost local proof can require owner-reviewed manual cleanup") and the summary ("evidence-backed surviving-journal recovery remain required") do not tell the owner this. The retired design handled fresh-host recovery deliberately (T16 restored mirrors, T53 "fresh-host recovery guidance"). The AC-05 proof has no control for an interrupted hosted job followed by a new scheduled run. This traces to the current revision's same-host rule and Q2 limit.
PROPOSAL: State the hosted run directory's lifetime and what happens after an interrupted hosted publication: the lease stays held, later scheduled runs report pending with the observed lease, and cleanup is manual. Put this consequence in Q2's cost column and the Owner summary. Add an AC-05/T125 control: a hosted job ends after lease acquisition, and the next scheduled run on a new runner writes nothing and reports the manual-cleanup handoff. If the owner wants hosted recovery, offer it as a Q2 option.

FINDING: F-02
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Installation and role execution", second and third paragraphs (component binding and ordinary plugin update; spec 0012 lines 29 and 31); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", first, third and fifth paragraphs (gate, successor checklist, upgrade table; spec 0015 lines 11, 13 and 17); Open Questions Q1 and Q3; Test Plan AC-07 (spec 0017 line 2); Tasks T122 and T130.
EVIDENCE: Before every production write, the gate requires "the installed payload fingerprint/version match". The fingerprint covers "the published plugin files". The code to reuse, computeComponentIdentity, hashes the whole `plugins/vh-adlc/` tree (bootstrap.ts:35 to 51). Ordinary installation follows the published main: `codex plugin add vh-adlc@vhco-tools` "resolve[s] the published main payload ... to `0.22.0`" (spec 0005 line 13), and the installed plugin "was refreshed to that published 0.23.0 version" (spec 0007 line 12). Upstream has since moved to 0.24.1 (spec 0002 line 3). Any upstream release, including one that changes only unrelated skills, changes the whole-plugin fingerprint. The gate then refuses production writes until the consumer lands "a reviewed successor checklist under the same order". The fixed upgrade table has no row for a component release that does not touch docs-drift, so "Unclassified changes remain pending for scoped review". The Design does not say how the scheduled wrapper or the interactive Codex/Claude entries install or select the exact bound release through "the ordinary marketplace installation". It also does not say how a rollback to the previous component version is installed, or whether a successor checklist must repeat all five preflight sections or only the selected upgrade rows. One implementer pins the hosted install to the bound release. Another installs latest and lets the gate block after every release. The AC-07 control "an altered installed source file under the same version" passes under both.
PROPOSAL: State how each entry obtains the exact bound release, or that the gate refuses and the consumer must update the binding. Add an upgrade-table row for component releases, separating releases that leave docs-drift unaffected from those that change it, and say which preflight sections a successor checklist repeats. Add an AC-07 control in which a newer marketplace release is installed while the old binding is committed. If this trade-off is the owner's call, raise it under Q1 or Q3.

FINDING: F-03
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", third paragraph (binding order; spec 0015 line 13) and fifth paragraph (upgrade table; spec 0015 line 17); Test Plan AC-07 ("acyclic C-to-D activation order", "unapproved activation changes fail"; spec 0017 line 2); Tasks T128 and T130.
EVIDENCE: The checklist records "the exact approved before/after activation-file hashes". After that, "The gate verifies that shape and the current normalized policy and activated files". Two runtime checks are left undefined. (1) The gate checks the activated files as they are now, and the only exemption is "Subsequent unrelated product changes". The activation diff covers the scheduled workflow. Under the pinned delivery convention, Renovate keeps action digests in workflows current ("Keep GitHub Actions digest pinning enabled", delivery.md 0002 lines 13 to 18). A routine digest bump to that workflow therefore changes an activated-file hash. No upgrade row classifies it, so production writes stop pending scoped review. (2) "D changes only the mode/checklist reference, adds that checklist and applies the recorded activation diff" does not say what D is compared against at runtime. The checklist can name C but cannot name D. Intervening default-branch commits, or delivery by merge commit (delivery.md 0001 lines 78 to 79), make diff(C, D) include unrelated changes unless D must be C's direct child. The retired design required "the exact direct-child record-only cutover shape" (T56). One implementer requires that D is C's direct child reachable from the current default and compares whole current files. Another checks only the normalized digest and checklist presence. Both pass the listed AC-07 controls.
PROPOSAL: State whether the runtime gate compares current activation files with the after-hashes. If it does, add an upgrade row for routine dependency and pin updates to those files. State the exact D shape the gate checks (for example, D is a direct child of C, and both are reachable from the current default) and how merge-commit delivery and intervening commits are handled. Add matching AC-07 controls.

FINDING: F-04
KIND: factual
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Acceptance Criteria AC-07 (spec 0016 line 13); Scope, third paragraph (spec 0011 line 11); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", sixth paragraph (spec 0015 line 21); Test Plan AC-06 and AC-07.
EVIDENCE: AC-07 says "Simpler installation, run/recovery and migration interfaces replace the private installer, receipt-chain and synthetic runtime machinery under the accepted tradeoffs". This revision's correction for R-87 F-09 changed Scope to "Keep the installed synthetic collector/harness needed to exercise actual installed code and model routes" (R87-corrections.patch 0002 lines 11 to 12). Design now says "Retain the accepted unattended installed synthetic trial ... The trial harness remains test-only". The criterion text was not updated. An implementation review must judge the exact accepted criterion text. One reviewer reads the retained installed synthetic harness as unreplaced "synthetic runtime machinery" and records AC-07 not met. Another reads the phrase as the test-only adoption gate and records it met. This traces to the current revision.
PROPOSAL: Revise AC-07 to match Scope, for example "replace the private installer, receipt-chain and retained runtime/adoption machinery, while the test-only installed synthetic harness stays outside production selection".

FINDING: F-05
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Collection, assessment and protected documents", second paragraph (spec 0013 line 13); Test Plan AC-03 (spec 0016 line 21); Tasks T124 (spec 0017 line 11).
EVIDENCE: The Design protects "HEAD, its branch/owned refs" and says "Actual file/index/ref changes still fail preservation checks", but it does not say which refs are compared. The shared snapshot offers two scopes. `full` digests every ref, and `owning` checks only HEAD and its branch because "other sessions move the shared refs" (review-route.ts:93 to 95). The current docs-drift capture digests every ref (snapshot.ts:260). With every ref compared, a commit in a sibling worktree (delivery.md 0001 lines 80 to 81 allows parallel worktrees), a user `git fetch`, or scheduled `git maintenance` prefetch writing `refs/prefetch/` changes the digest. The run then reports caller-changed, which contradicts the AC-03 requirement that "background git maintenance ... must leave collection complete". With the owning scope, a write to any other caller ref goes undetected, but the AC-03 control "Real ... ref ... mutation must fail preservation" names no ref. Both implementations pass the listed controls.
PROPOSAL: State which caller refs preservation compares, for example HEAD and its current branch only, with remote-tracking and `refs/prefetch/` refs excluded. Add AC-03/T124 controls: a sibling-worktree commit and a maintenance prefetch during collection leave collection complete, and a write to the caller's current branch fails preservation.

FINDING: F-06
KIND: judgement
SEVERITY: should-fix
WHERE: Design "Publication, concurrency and recovery", second paragraph (spec 0013 line 17); Test Plan AC-05 ("copied-run observation-only refusals"; spec 0016 line 23); Tasks T125 (spec 0017 line 12).
EVIDENCE: Copy detection depends on "the platform machine ID/UUID" together with the canonical directory. Machine IDs are not unique across cloned VM or container images that bake in one value, and dev containers built from one image usually share their paths. On such a host, a byte copy of the run directory matches both checks and is treated as the origin. The copy also carries the original journal's run nonce, which is what proves lease ownership (T46), and attempt claims are revision-CAS writes in the local journal copy (attempts.ts:89 to 109). The original and the copy can therefore both claim and dispatch the next effect. That breaks AC-05's "never repeats a completed write" and the Design's rule that a copy is observation-only. The Design handles an unavailable machine ID but not a duplicated one, and no AC-05 control covers it. This traces to the current revision's response to R-87 F-04.
PROPOSAL: Bind the origin claim to something a directory copy does not carry, for example a host-local record outside the run directory created at run start, or state the accepted risk explicitly. Add an AC-05/T125 control in which a copy with the same reported machine ID and path stays observation-only.

WHERE: Context "Owner summary", fourth paragraph (spec 0001 line 81); Design "Migration, compatibility and rollout", fourth paragraph (spec 0015 line 15); Context "Terms used here", Migration checklist row (spec 0002 line 24).
EVIDENCE: The summary says "Migration uses a recorded checklist of exact release/install, scanner parity, Claude Code Opus 5.5 paired review, actual model/entry tests, a successful unattended installed synthetic trial, routing switch and rollback". The Design says the preflight checklist "cannot contain the future actual routing-switch outcome", which is observed "in a separate delivery report". The Terms row agrees with the Design. This revision changed the Design and the Terms row for R-87 F-06 but left "routing switch" in the summary's checklist list. Nothing an implementer builds changes.
PROPOSAL: Change the summary to "a recorded preflight checklist of exact release/install, scanner parity, final Claude Code Opus 5.5 paired review, actual model/entry tests, a successful unattended installed synthetic trial and rollback rehearsal, with the routing switch reported separately after activation".

## Sources

- GitHub REST users reference, read 2026-11-15. Durable numeric-ID lookup supports GitHub App installation tokens and returns a native login and numeric ID. [Get a user using their ID](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/users/users#get-a-user-using-their-id).

- [GitHub REST rate limits](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api). The ordinary built-in Actions-token allowance motivates the native closure quota regression in T105.
- [Git revision traversal](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-list)
- [GitHub workflow runs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs) and [GitHub CLI run listing](https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_run_list) identify native run status and conclusion values used in quiescence records.

- [curl manual](https://curl.se/docs/manpage.html) documents transfer/connection limits, disabled retries and `-q`. The installer makes one bounded attempt per route and excludes user curl configuration.

- [Earlier pinned feature-document standard](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/feature-documents.md) and [template](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/feature-document-template.md): one child document, owner decisions, review and status gates.
- [Earlier pinned documentation conventions](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/documentation.md), [delivery rules](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/conventions/delivery.md) and [prose guide](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/guides/humanize-prose.md): publication boundaries, authoritative local rules and concise owner-facing prose.
- [Earlier pinned agent entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/0e52f51f01b84eda75a976763c42d8ec0a07b544/AGENTS.md): source-grounded task routing and retained consumer instructions.
- Stackweaver files cited in Context were inspected locally at `d77259358c87aebc93ad76231ae245fd790c043a`; private report bodies, application source and secrets are not copied into this draft.

- [GitHub local plugin directories](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-sdk/features/plugin-directories), [CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference) and [programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference), consulted 2026-11-05: explicit plugin loading, isolated configuration and update controls. Local CLI help confirms `--plugin-dir`, `COPILOT_HOME` and automatic-update controls; actual new-operation loading remains a required test.
- [GitHub Actions artifacts REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/artifacts): artifact IDs, expiry/digest/run metadata, archive retrieval and Actions read permission for private resources used by the proposed history reader.
- [GitHub Actions workflow runs REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-runs): native workflow/event/branch/head/attempt metadata used to distinguish adopted scheduled producer runs from other workflows and pull-request runs.
- [yauzl 3.4.0 source](https://github.com/thejoshwolfe/yauzl/tree/f5798e15204ffbb9a428b00217e4659684a41b0f): exact tagged ZIP-library source selected for bundling; its documented buffer/lazy-entry/filename/size-validation APIs support the proposed bounded wrapper. Package installation and integrity remain implementation verification.

- [Official yauzl 3.4.0 npm metadata](https://registry.npmjs.org/yauzl/3.4.0) and [Bun 1.4.4 release](https://github.com/oven-sh/bun/releases/tag/bun-v1.4.4). T10 records registry integrity, exact upstream-source comparison and verified Linux build-tool execution.

- [GitHub workflow jobs API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/actions/workflow-jobs). T12 uses the fixed run-attempt jobs endpoint and native step outcomes under the existing Actions read permission. The run API examples also document branch-qualified workflow paths, which are checked against the recorded default branch or exact native head.

- [Earlier engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/AGENTS.md) and [owner-summary guidance](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/f781ee7b0404e80194aa568af3284888cc222017/conventions/feature-documents.md). T40 integrates the published upstream pin and adds a summary and glossary of the accepted behavior.

GitHub documents that the actual `merge_commit_sha` after a merge identifies the resulting merge commit, squashed commit or rebased default tip. T51 reads that identity separately from a hypothetical pre-merge test commit. [GitHub pull-request REST documentation](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/pulls/pulls#get-a-pull-request)

Git documents all-or-none ref updates for an atomic push and failure when the server lacks that capability. T52 verifies those outcomes against an owned local receive-pack server. [Git push documentation](https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push)

- [GitHub labels REST API](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/issues/labels#get-a-label) provides the repository label-identity lookup used to compare native canonical spelling. Read-only GETs for `cli/cli` labels `bug` and `BUG` returned the same label ID and canonical name during implementation.

- [Git reference reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/refs#get-a-reference) and [Git commit object reads](https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/commits#get-a-commit-object) use repository Contents read permission. Native ref absence rechecks the current publication repository identity, default ref and native commit, retaining those requests before classifying visible absence.

- Bun's archive API provides in-memory regular-file reads and tar extraction. [Bun archive documentation](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive).
- Copilot exposes explicit configuration/cache locations and update suppression for scoped probes. [Copilot CLI configuration directory](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-config-dir-reference), [Copilot programmatic reference](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/reference/copilot-cli-reference/cli-programmatic-reference).
- Claude exposes separate configuration and update controls for the version-only probe. [Claude Code environment variables](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/env-vars).

The bounded client-distribution reader uses Bun for regular tar files and the runtime zlib API for capped gzip expansion. It validates ustar metadata separately so omitted entries cannot become an accepted package. Extended archive formats require an explicitly supported setup path. [Bun archive reference](https://bun.sh/docs/runtime/archive), [Node zlib options](https://nodejs.org/api/zlib.html#class-options)

Non-executing client upgrade signals use the documented provider npm package names as intake identifiers. Runtime selection continues to use independently pinned native distributions. [Copilot CLI installation](https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/get-started/cli-quickstart), [Claude Code setup](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/setup), [Codex source installation guide](https://github.com/openai/codex#installation)

- The earlier T40 integration selected `d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab` for its recorded checkpoints. [Engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/AGENTS.md), [terminal output](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/conventions/terminal-output.md), [delivery rules](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/d8f07c9dd69038375a535af8ad586086688f85ab/conventions/delivery.md).
- T106 selects the current engineering pin `1ccf8f314575ece1f3fcc8e48c4fd804a1ca88ff`. [Current engineering entry point](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/1ccf8f314575ece1f3fcc8e48c4fd804a1ca88ff/AGENTS.md), [feature-document standard](https://github.com/vhco-pro/docs/blob/1ccf8f314575ece1f3fcc8e48c4fd804a1ca88ff/conventions/feature-documents.md).
